a
a
Q_{0}
Q_{2}
Q^{\prime}_{2}
Q_{1}
Q_{3}
{\rm t}
a
\tau
\tau
a
R_{0}
R_{2}
R^{\prime}_{2}
R_{1}
{\rm t}
a
\tau
\tau

Figure 3:
Counter-Example to the Absence of a Stability Respecting Clause

In Figure
3
, three processes are represented as LTSs. Take
A:=\{a\}
. According to Definition
1
,
\neg(P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{0})
and
Q_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R_{0}
.

Let’s try to build a concrete branching reactive bisimulation between the top-left and bottom processes. Matching the time-out between
Q_{0}
and
Q_{2}
implies that
Q_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c\emptyset}_{br}\!}P_{0}
or
Q_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c\emptyset}_{br}\!}P_{2}
. However,
P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
P_{2}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, thus, there should be a path
Q_{2}\Longrightarrow Q_{2}^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, but this is not the case.

The symmetric closure of

\displaystyle\R:=\{(Q_{0},R_{0}),(Q_{1},R_{1}),(Q_{2},\emptyset,R_{2}),(Q_{2}^{\prime},\emptyset,R_{2}^{\prime})\}\cup\{(Q_{0},X,R_{0}),(Q_{1},X,R_{1})\mid X\subseteq A\}

is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. The
a
-transition between
Q_{2}^{\prime}
and
Q_{3}
does not have to be matched since
Q_{2}^{\prime}
is considered only when the environment disallows
a
.

Now, suppose that the stability respecting condition is removed from Definition
1
. As a result, a concrete branching reactive bisimulation can be built between the top-left and bottom processes. The symmetric closure of

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle\{(P_{0},Q_{0}),(P_{1},Q_{1}),(P_{2},Q_{2}),(P_{2},Q_{2}^{\prime}),(P_{3},Q_{3})\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(P_{0},X,Q_{0}),(P_{1},X,Q_{1}),(P_{2},X,Q_{2}),(P_{2},X,Q_{2}^{\prime}),(P_{3},X,Q_{3})\mid X\subseteq A\}

would be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Moreover,
\R
would still be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation, since Definition
1
has merely been weakened. Therefore, according to the modified Definition
1
,
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
and
Q_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R_{0}
. However, when trying to construct a concrete branching reactive bisimulation between
P_{0}
and
R_{0}
, because of the time-out transition,
R_{2}
has to be matched to
P_{0}
or
P_{2}
and no
a
-transition is reachable from
R_{2}
; therefore,
\neg(P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R_{0})
. As a result, removing the stability respecting clause from Definition
1
prevents
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
from being an equivalence relation.

Appendix B
Generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation

The second clause of Definition
1
is quite tedious to check; thus, an equivalent definition of the bisimilarity would be useful. Actually, it is possible to define the exact same notion in a more general way at the cost of some clear motivations.

Definition 32
.

A
generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation
is a symmetric relation
\R\subseteq(\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P})\cup(\mathbb{P}\times\mathcal{P}(A)\times\mathbb{P})
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
,

1.

if
\R(P,Q)

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
,

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
,

(c)

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
;

2.

if
\R(P,X,Q)

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
,

(b)

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
with
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
,

(c)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},Y,Q_{2})
,

(d)

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

The strong point of the generalised definitions is the restriction on the use of triplets, making use of them only after performing a time-out. A generalised version of rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation can be defined in a similar fashion.

Definition 33
.

A
generalised rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation
is a symmetric relation
\R\subseteq(\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P})\cup(\mathbb{P}\times\mathcal{P}(A)\times\mathbb{P})
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
,

1.

if
\R(P,Q)

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
,

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
with
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
,

2.

if
\R(P,X,Q)

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
,

(b)

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
,

(c)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cY}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

Note that if a system has no time-out, then a generalised [rooted] concrete branching reactive bisimulation is a stability respecting [rooted] branching bisimulation, thus proving that [rooted] concrete branching reactive bisimilarity is indeed an extension of stability respecting [rooted] branching bisimilarity to reactive systems with time-outs.

Proposition 34
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
,

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
(resp.
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
) iff there exists a generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation
\R
with
\R(P,Q)
(resp.
\R(P,X,Q)
),

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
(resp.
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
) iff there exists a rooted generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation
\R
with
\R(P,Q)
(resp.
\R(P,X,Q)
).

Proof.

Let
\R
be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let’s check that it is a generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R(P,Q)

(a)

this condition is shared by both definitions

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
\R(P,Q)
,
\R(P,X,Q)
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(c)

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, since
\R(P,Q)
,
\R(P,\emptyset,Q)
, so there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

2.

If
\R(P,X,Q)

(a)

this condition is shared by both definitions

(b)

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then if
a\in X
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Otherwise,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and so
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, thus there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R(P,X,Q)
,
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}
,
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
. As
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
\R(P,Q_{1})
. Because
P\xrightarrow{a}P_{1}
and
Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a transition
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. As a result, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Furthermore, using Lemma
2
.1,
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, thus,
\R(P,Q_{1})
and so
\R(P,Y,Q_{1})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q_{1}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},Y,Q_{2})
. Since
Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
Q_{1}=Q_{1}^{\prime}
. As a result, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},Y,Q_{2})
.

(d)

this condition is shared by both definitions.

Let
\R
be a generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation and define

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=\R

\displaystyle\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\R(P,Q)\wedge X\subseteq A\}\cup\{(P,Y,Q),(P,Q)\mid\exists X\subseteq A,\;\R(P,X,Q)

\displaystyle\wedge(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\wedge Y\subseteq A\}

\R^{\prime}
is symmetric by definition. Let’s check that
\R^{\prime}
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
then
\R(P,Q)
or there exists a set
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,Q_{2})
and, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})

•

if there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, since
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
\alpha\neq\tau
, so there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Y,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,Q_{2})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
Q=Q_{1}
so there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}
such that
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(b)

For all
Z\subseteq A
,

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Z,Q)

•

if there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Z,Q)
.

2.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q)
then
\R(P,X,Q)
, or
\R(P,Q)
, or there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then

•

if
\R(P,X,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,X,Q_{2})
and, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,Q_{2})
and, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})

•

if there exists

such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so this case is impossible.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
then

•

if
\R(P,X,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,Q_{2})
and, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P,Q_{2})
and, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})

•

if there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Y,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
Q=Q_{1}
so there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q)
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then

•

if
\R(P,X,Q)
then, since
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By Clause 2.a of Definition
32
,
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, therefore, by definition,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{0})
.

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

•

if there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

(d)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and

then

•

if
\R(P,X,Q)
then, there exists a path

with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Hence also
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Hence also

•

if there exists

and

then
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((Y\cup X)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
so there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Hence also
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(e)

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then

•

if
\R(P,X,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

if there exists
Y\subseteq A
such that
\R(P,Y,Q)
and
(\mathcal{I}(P)\cup\mathcal{I}(Q))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
Q\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Let
\R
be a rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let’s check that it is a generalised rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and

If

(a)

this condition is shared by both definitions

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and

\R(P,Q)

\R(P,X,Q)
. Since

P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}

(a)

this condition is shared by both definitions

(b)

if

.

Appendix C
Pohlmann Encoding

Reactive bisimulations are sometimes complicated to check because of the large number of potential sets of allowed actions. In
[
19
]
, Pohlmann introduces an encoding which reduces strong reactive bisimilarity to strong bisimilarity. To this end he introduces unary operators
\vartheta
and
\vartheta_{X}
for
X\subseteq A

that model placing their argument process in an environment that is triggered to change, or allows
exactly the actions in
X
, respectively. The actions
{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}\notin A
and
\varepsilon_{X}\notin A
for
X\subseteq A
are generated by the new operators, but may not be used by processes substituted for their arguments
P
.
They model a time-out action taken by the environment, and the stabilisation of an environment into
one that allows exactly the set of actions
X
, respectively. After a slight modification of the encoding, a similar result can be obtained for concrete branching reactive bisimilarity and its rooted version.

\begin{array}[]{l l l}\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\vartheta(P^{\prime})&\Leftrightarrow&P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\\
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\vartheta_{X}(P)&&\\
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})&\Leftrightarrow&P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}\\
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(P^{\prime})&\Leftrightarrow&P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in X\\
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta(P)&\Leftrightarrow&\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\\
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})&\Leftrightarrow&\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\wedge P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}\end{array}

Table 4:
Operational semantics of
\vartheta
and
(\vartheta_{X})_{X\subseteq A}

In
[
19
]
, the first rule only applies to
\tau
-transitions; this echoes the previous remark about applying the first clause of Definition
1
only to invisible actions. Note that the encoding rules mirror the clauses of Definition
1
. The encoding transforms
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
into
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}
, and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
in
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}
.

Proposition 35
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
.

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q\Leftrightarrow\vartheta(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q)

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q\Leftrightarrow\vartheta(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q)

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q\Leftrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta_{X}(Q)

•

P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q\Leftrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}\vartheta_{X}(Q)

Proof.

It suffices to prove that: if
\R
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation then
\R^{\prime}:=\{(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\mid\R(P,Q)\}\cup\{(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\mid\R(P,X,Q)\}
is a stability respecting branching bisimulation; and if
\R
is a stability respecting branching bisimulation then
\R^{\prime}:=\{(P,Q),(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\wedge X\subseteq A\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\}
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. The rooted case is very similar.

Let
\R
be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation and define

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=\{(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\mid\R(P,Q)\}\cup\{(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\mid\R(P,X,Q)\}

We are going to check that
\R^{\prime}
is a stability respecting branching bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

•

If
P=\vartheta(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\vartheta(Q^{\dagger})
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}\cup\{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon_{X}\mid X\subseteq A\}
then

–

if
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\star})
and
\R(P^{\ddagger},Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta(Q^{\star})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,\vartheta(Q^{\star}))
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger}))
.

–

if
\alpha={\rm t}_{\varepsilon}
then this case is not possible according to the semantics of
\vartheta
.

–

if
\alpha=\varepsilon_{X}
with
X\subseteq A
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
,
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
. By the semantics,
Q\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
such that, by the definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}))
.

2.

If
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics, this is impossible.

3.

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta
,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
,
\R(P^{\dagger},\emptyset,Q^{\dagger})
, so there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta(Q^{\star})\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

If there exists
X\subseteq A
such that
P=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}\cup\{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon_{X}\mid X\subseteq A\}
then

–

if
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\star})
and
\R(P^{\ddagger},X,Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star}))
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger}))
.

–

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in A
then, by the semantics,
a\in X
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\star})
and
\R(P^{\ddagger},Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star})\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star}))
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger}))
.

–

if
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\dagger})
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Moreover,
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\star})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\star})
and
Q^{\star}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\star})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\star})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta(Q^{\star})
such that, by the definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\star}))
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta(Q^{\star}))
.

–

if
\alpha=\varepsilon_{X}
with
X\subseteq A
then this case is impossible according to the semantics of
\vartheta_{X}
.

2.

if
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, according to Lemma
2
.4, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})=\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger}_{1})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
and
Q^{\dagger}_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
with
\R(P^{\ddagger},X,Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. According to the semantics,
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(Q_{2}^{\dagger})
and, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta_{X}(Q_{2}^{\dagger}))
.

3.

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta_{X}
,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Let
\R
be a stability respecting branching bisimulation and define

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle\{(P,Q),(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\wedge X\subseteq A\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\}

We are going to show that
\R^{\prime}
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
then
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
. Since
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
, there exists a path
\vartheta(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(\vartheta(P),Q^{\star})
and
\R(\vartheta(P^{\prime}),Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta(Q_{1})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q_{2})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(b)

For all
Y\subseteq A
, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Y,Q)
.

2.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q)
then
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
or
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
. If
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
then
\vartheta(P)\!\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\vartheta_{X}(P)
, thus there exists a path
\vartheta(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(\vartheta(P),Q^{\star})
and
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta(Q_{0})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})
. Since
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),Q^{\star})
and
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime}),Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{1})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{2})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
. As
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),Q^{\star})
and
\R(\vartheta(P^{\prime}),Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{1})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q_{2})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta(P)
. As
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),Q^{\star})
and
\R(\vartheta(P),Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}^{\prime})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q_{0}^{\prime})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{0}^{\prime})
.

(d)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})
. Since
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
with
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime}),Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that
Q^{\dagger}_{1}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{1})
,
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q^{\dagger}_{2}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{2})
. Thus, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(e)

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0}))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q_{0})\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\star}=\vartheta_{X}(Q_{1})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Let
\R
be a rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation and define

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=\{(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\mid\R(P,Q)\}\cup\{(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\mid\R(P,X,Q)\}

We are going to check that
\R^{\prime}
is a rooted stability respecting branching bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

•

If
P=\vartheta(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\vartheta(Q^{\dagger})
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
.

1.

Let
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in Act\cup\{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon_{X}\mid X\mathop{\subseteq}A\}
.

–

If
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the first part of this proof,
\vartheta(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
.

–

The case
\alpha={\rm t}_{\varepsilon}
is not possible according to the semantics of
\vartheta
.

–

If
\alpha=\varepsilon_{X}
with
X\subseteq A
then, by the semantics of
\vartheta
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
,
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q^{\dagger}
so
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\dagger}
. By the semantics,
Q\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
such that, by the first part of this proof,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
.

–

The case
\alpha={\rm t}
, by the semantics, is not possible.

•

If there exists
X\subseteq A
such that
P=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
.

1.

Let
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in Act\cup\{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon_{X}\mid X\mathop{\subseteq}A\}
.

–

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the first part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
.

–

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in A
then, by the semantics,
a\in X
,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the first part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q^{\ddagger})
.

–

If
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta(P^{\dagger})
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta(Q^{\dagger})
such that, by the definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},\vartheta(Q^{\dagger}))
. Considering the previous case, this implies that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q^{\dagger})
and so
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta(Q^{\dagger})
.

–

The case
\alpha=\varepsilon_{X}
with
X\subseteq A
is impossible according to the semantics of
\vartheta_{X}
.

–

If
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\vartheta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
such that, by the first part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
.

Let
\R
be a rooted stability respecting branching bisimulation and define

\R^{\prime}:={\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}}\,\cup\{(P,Q),(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))\wedge X\subseteq A\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))\}

We are going to show that
\R^{\prime}
is a rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
then
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
. Since
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
, there exists a transition
\vartheta(Q)\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

For all
Y\subseteq A
, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Y,Q)
.

2.

If
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q)
then
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
or
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
and
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})
.

•

If
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
, there exists a path
\vartheta(Q)\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
so that, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
and thus
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

If
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
so that, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{a}\vartheta(P^{\prime})
.

•

If
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
, there exists a step
\vartheta(Q)\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

If
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
, there exists a path
\vartheta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then

•

if
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
then, by definition of
\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

•

if
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}_{\varepsilon}}\vartheta(P)
. As
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
,
\vartheta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{t_{\varepsilon}}Q^{\prime}
with
\vartheta(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\prime}
and, by the semantics,
Q^{\prime}=\vartheta(Q)
, thus, by the second part,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
by Lemma
2
.3, and so
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

(d)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})
.

•

If
\R(\vartheta(P),\vartheta(Q))
then, since
\vartheta(P)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}\vartheta_{X}(P)
, there exists a transition
\vartheta(Q)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{X}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
\vartheta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\dagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\dagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q)
. Since
P\!\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, also
\vartheta(P)\!\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so
\vartheta(Q)\!\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
Q\!\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Moreover, since
\vartheta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})
and
Q\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a transition
\vartheta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
, thus, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

if
\R(\vartheta_{X}(P),\vartheta_{X}(Q))
then there exists a transition
\vartheta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
\vartheta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\vartheta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
. Moreover, by the second part,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Stuttering Property and Transitivity

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Let
\R
be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let’s define

\displaystyle\R^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle\{(P^{\dagger},Q),(Q,P^{\dagger})\mid\exists P,P^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{P},P\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P^{\ddagger}\wedge\R(P,Q)\wedge\R(P^{\ddagger},Q)\}\cup{}

\displaystyle\{(P^{\dagger}\!\!,X,Q),(Q,X,P^{\dagger})\mid\exists P,P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P},P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}\wedge\R(P,X,Q)\wedge\R(P^{\ddagger}\!\!,X,Q)\}

\R^{\prime}
is symmetric by definition and we are going to prove that
\R^{\prime}
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Note that
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
(by taking
P^{\ddagger}=P^{\dagger}
). Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

Let
\R^{\prime}(P,Q)
.

(a)

Suppose
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P\Longrightarrow P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger},Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger},Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,Q_{0})
. Since
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(b)

For all
Y\subseteq A
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Y,Q)
by definition of
\R^{\prime}
.

2.

Let
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q)
.

(a)

Suppose
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger}\!,X,Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger}\!,X,Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
. Since
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(b)

Suppose
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger}\!,X,Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger}\!,X,Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
. Since
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

Suppose
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger}\!,X,Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger}\!,X,Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\R(P,Q_{0}^{\prime})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q^{\prime}_{0})
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,Q_{0})
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P,Q_{0})
.

(d)

Suppose
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger}\!,X,Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger}\!,X,Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that, since
\R\subseteq\R^{\prime}
,
\R^{\prime}(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(e)

Suppose
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

Let there exist
P^{\dagger},P^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P^{\dagger}\!,X,Q)
and
\R(P^{\ddagger}\!,X,Q)
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q)
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
. Since
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

Let there exist
Q^{\dagger},Q^{\ddagger}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}
such that
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}Q^{\ddagger}
,
\R(P,X,Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
. Since
\R(P,X,Q^{\ddagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4
.

Let
\R_{1}
and
\R_{2}
be two concrete branching reactive bisimulations and define

\displaystyle\R:=(\R_{1}\circ\R_{2})\cup(\R_{2}\circ\R_{1})

\R
is clearly symmetric by definition. Let’s check that
\R
is a concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R(P,Q)
then there exists
R\in\mathbb{P}
such that
\R_{1}(P,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,Q)
, or
\R_{2}(P,R)
and
\R_{1}(R,Q)
. The two possibilities are similar; thus, suppose without loss of generality that
\R_{1}(P,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,Q)
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, since
\R_{1}(P,R)
, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}R_{2}
such that
\R_{1}(P,R_{1})
and
\R_{1}(P^{\prime},R_{2})
. Since
\R_{2}(R,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},Q_{0})
. Since
R_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}R_{2}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},Q_{1})
and
\R_{2}(R_{2},Q_{2})
. By definition of
\R
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(b)

For all
Y\subseteq A
, since
\R_{1}(P,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,Q)
,
\R_{1}(P,Y,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,Y,Q)
, thus,
\R(P,Y,Q)
.

2.

If
\R(P,X,Q)
then there exists
R\in\mathbb{P}
such that
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
, or
\R_{2}(P,X,R)
and
\R_{1}(R,X,Q)
. The two possibilities are similar; thus, suppose without loss of generality that
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
and
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, since
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}R_{2}
such that
\R_{1}(P,X,R_{1})
and
\R_{1}(P^{\prime},X,R_{2})
. Since
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},X,Q_{0})
. Since
R_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}R_{2}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},X,Q_{1})
and
\R_{2}(R_{2},X,Q_{2})
. By definition of
\R
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
then, since
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}\xrightarrow{a}R_{2}
such that
\R_{1}(P,X,R_{1})
and
\R_{1}(P^{\prime},R_{2})
. Since
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},X,Q_{0})
. Since
R_{1}\xrightarrow{a}R_{2}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},X,Q_{1})
and
\R_{2}(R_{2},Q_{2})
. By definition of
\R
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, since
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Moreover, using Clause 2.a,
\R_{1}(P,X,R_{0})
. Moreover, there exists a path
R_{0}\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}_{0}
such that
\R_{1}(P,R^{\prime}_{0})
, but, since
R_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
R_{0}=R^{\prime}_{0}
. By Clause 1.a,
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(R_{0})
, so
\mathcal{I}(R_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{0}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{0},X,Q_{0})
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{I}(R_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{0},Q^{\prime}_{0})
. Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,Q^{\prime}_{0})
.

(d)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
, according to Lemma
2
.4, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
\mathcal{I}(R_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\R_{1}(P,X,R_{1})
. Moreover, there exists a transition
R_{1}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}}R_{2}
such that
\R_{1}(P^{\prime}\!,X,R_{2})
. Since
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{1}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{1},X,Q_{0})
. Since
\mathcal{I}(R_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
R_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R_{2}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R_{2}(R_{2},X,Q_{2})
. As a result, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(e)

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, since
\R_{1}(P,X,R)
, there exists a path
R\Longrightarrow R_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R_{2}(R,X,Q)
and
R\Longrightarrow R_{0}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
\R_{2}(R_{0},X,Q_{0})
. Since
R_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Hence there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of Modal Characterisation

Proof of Theorem
11
.

(\Rightarrow)
We are going to prove by structural induction on
\mathbb{L}_{b}
and
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
,
X\subseteq A
,
\varphi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}
and
\psi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
,

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models\varphi
then
Q\models\varphi

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models_{X}\varphi
then
Q\models_{X}\varphi

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models\psi
then
Q\models\psi

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models_{X}\psi
then
Q\models_{X}\psi

Note that, in the four cases, we dispose of the contraposition. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
,
X\subseteq A
,
\varphi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}
and
\psi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models\varphi
then

–

if
\varphi=\top
then
Q\models\top
.

–

if
\varphi=\bigwedge_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
with
(\varphi_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{L}_{b})^{I}
then, for all
i\in I
,
P\models\varphi_{i}
. Thus, by induction, for all
i\in I
,
Q\models\varphi_{i}
. Therefore,
Q\models\bigwedge_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
.

–

if
\varphi=\neg\varphi^{\prime}
then
P\not\models\varphi^{\prime}
. Thus, by induction,
Q\not\models\varphi^{\prime}
. Therefore,
Q\models\neg\varphi^{\prime}
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi_{1}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi_{2})
then there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\models\varphi_{1}
and
P_{2}\models\varphi_{2}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By induction,
Q_{1}\models\varphi_{1}
and
Q_{2}\models\varphi_{2}
. Therefore,
Q\models\varphi
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle X\rangle\varphi
then there is a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P_{2}\models_{X}\varphi_{2}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
(cf. Lemma
2
.4). Thus, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By induction,
Q_{2}\models_{X}\varphi
so
Q\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle X\rangle\varphi
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
then there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Therefore,
Q\models\varphi
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models_{X}\varphi
then

–

if
\varphi=\top
then
Q\models_{X}\top
.

–

if
\varphi=\bigwedge_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
with
(\varphi_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{L}_{b})^{I}
then, for all
i\in I
,
P\models_{X}\varphi_{i}
. Thus, by induction, for all
i\in I
,
Q\models_{X}\varphi_{i}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\bigwedge_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
.

–

if
\varphi=\neg\varphi^{\prime}
then
P\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\prime}
. Thus, by induction,
Q\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\prime}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\neg\varphi^{\prime}
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi_{1}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi_{2})
then

*

if
\alpha=\tau
then there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\models_{X}\varphi_{1}
and
P_{2}\models_{X}\varphi_{2}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By induction,
Q_{1}\models_{X}\varphi_{1}
and
Q_{2}\models_{X}\varphi_{2}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\varphi
.

*

if
\alpha\in A
then
a\in X
or
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{a}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\models_{X}\varphi_{1}
and
P_{2}\models\varphi_{2}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. Moreover, with Lemma
2
.4 we can get that
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By induction,
Q_{1}\models_{X}\varphi_{1}
and
Q_{2}\models\varphi_{2}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\varphi
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle Y\rangle\varphi
then there is a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P_{2}\models_{Y}\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
, and by Lemma
2
.4 there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}
such that
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
, and hence
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cY}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
. Thus, there exists a transition
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cY}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By induction,
Q_{2}\models_{Y}\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle Y\rangle\varphi
.

–

if
\varphi=\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
then there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Therefore,
Q\models\varphi
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models\psi
then

–

if
\psi=\top
then
Q\models\top
.

–

if
\psi=\bigwedge_{i\in I}\psi_{i}
with
(\psi_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r})^{I}
then, for all
i\in I
,
P\models\psi_{i}
. Thus, by induction, for all
i\in I
,
Q\models\psi_{i}
. Therefore,
Q\models\bigwedge_{i\in I}\psi_{i}
.

–

if
\psi=\neg\psi^{\prime}
then
P\not\models\psi^{\prime}
. Thus, by induction,
Q\not\models\psi^{\prime}
. Therefore,
Q\models\neg\psi^{\prime}
.

–

if
\psi=\langle\alpha\rangle\varphi
then there is a transition
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\models\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By induction,
Q^{\prime}\models\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models\psi
.

–

if
\psi=\langle X\rangle\varphi
then
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
,
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By induction,
Q^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models\psi
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
and
P\models_{X}\psi
then

–

if
\psi=\top
then
Q\models_{X}\top
.

–

if
\psi=\bigwedge_{i\in I}\psi_{i}
with
(\psi_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r})^{I}
then, for all
i\in I
,
P\models_{X}\psi_{i}
. Thus, by induction, for all
i\in I
,
Q\models_{X}\psi_{i}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\bigwedge_{i\in I}\psi_{i}
.

–

if
\psi=\neg\psi^{\prime}
then
P\not\models_{X}\psi^{\prime}
. Thus, by induction,
Q\not\models_{X}\psi^{\prime}
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\neg\psi^{\prime}
.

–

if
\psi=\langle\alpha\rangle\varphi

*

if
\alpha=\tau
then there exists a transition
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By induction,
Q^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\psi
.

*

if
\alpha\in A
then
a\in X
or
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\models\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By induction,
Q^{\prime}\models\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\psi
.

–

if
\psi=\langle Y\rangle\varphi
then
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
such that
P_{1}\models_{Y}\varphi
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{crX}_{br}\!}Q
,
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cY}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By induction,
Q^{\prime}\models_{Y}\varphi
. Therefore,
Q\models_{X}\psi
.

(\Leftarrow)
Let
\equiv\;:=\{(P,Q)\mid\forall\varphi\in\mathbb{L}^{c}_{b},P\models\varphi\Leftrightarrow Q\models\varphi\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\forall\varphi\in\mathbb{L}^{c}_{b},P\models_{X}\varphi\Leftrightarrow Q\models_{X}\varphi\}
, and
\equiv^{r}\;:=\{(P,Q)\mid\forall\psi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{cr},P\models\psi\Leftrightarrow Q\models\psi\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\forall\psi\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{cr},P\models_{X}\psi\Leftrightarrow Q\models_{X}\psi\}
.
(P,X,Q)\in{\equiv}
will be denoted
P\equiv_{X}Q
for clarity. Note that
{\equiv^{r}}\subseteq{\equiv}
. We are going to check that
\equiv
is a generalised concrete branching bisimulation and
\equiv^{r}
a generalised rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
P\equiv Q

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}:=\{Q^{\dagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\wedge P\not\equiv Q^{\dagger}\}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist two formulas
\varphi^{\dagger},\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}
such that
P\models\varphi^{\dagger}
,
P^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
, for all
Q^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
,
Q^{\dagger}\not\models\varphi^{\dagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Thus,
Q\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
Q_{1}\models\varphi^{\dagger}
and
Q_{2}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P\equiv Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\equiv Q_{2}
.

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}:=\{Q_{2}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{X}Q_{2}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{c}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exists a formula
\varphi\in\mathbb{L}^{c}_{b}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi\!
and, for all
Q\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{Q}
,
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\not\models_{X}}\varphi
. Note that
P\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle X\rangle\varphi
. Thus,
Q\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle X\rangle\varphi
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q_{2}\models_{X}\varphi
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv_{X}Q_{2}
.

(c)

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then
P\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
. Thus
Q\models\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
. Therefore,
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

2.

If
P\equiv_{X}Q
then

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}:=\{Q^{\dagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\wedge P\not\equiv_{X}Q^{\dagger}\}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}\linebreak\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{X}Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist two formulas
\varphi^{\dagger},\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}
such that
P\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
,
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
, for all
Q^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
,
Q^{\dagger}\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\tau}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\tau}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
Q_{1}\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
and
Q_{2}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P\equiv_{X}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\equiv_{X}Q_{2}
.

(b)

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X
or
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}:=\{Q^{\dagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\linebreak\wedge P\not\equiv_{X}Q^{\dagger}\}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist two formulas
\varphi^{\dagger},\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}
such that
P\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
,
P^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
, for all
Q^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
,
Q^{\dagger}\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle(\varphi^{\dagger}\langle\hat{\alpha}\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger})
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
Q_{1}\models_{X}\varphi^{\dagger}
and
Q_{2}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P\equiv_{X}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\equiv Q_{2}
.

(c)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}:=\{Q_{2}\mid Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}\linebreak\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{Y}Q_{2}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{c}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exists a formula
\varphi\in\mathbb{L}^{c}_{b}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{Y}\varphi\!
and, for all
Q\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{Q}
,
Q^{\dagger}\mathbin{\not\models_{Y}}\varphi
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle Y\rangle\varphi
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle\langle Y\rangle\varphi
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
Q_{2}\models_{Y}\varphi
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv_{Y}Q_{2}
.

(d)

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then
P\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
. Thus
Q\models_{X}\langle\varepsilon\rangle\neg\langle\tau\rangle\top
. Therefore,
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

1.

If
P\equiv^{r}Q
then

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist a formula
\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
such that
P^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models\langle\alpha\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Thus,
Q\models\langle\alpha\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Therefore, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{X}Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist a formula
\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models\langle X\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Thus,
Q\models\langle X\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Therefore, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv_{X}Q^{\prime}
.

2.

If
P\equiv^{r}_{X}Q
then

(a)

if
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{X}Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
