is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist a formula
\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle\tau\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle\tau\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Therefore, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\models_{X}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv_{X}Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

if
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist a formula
\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
such that
P^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle a\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle a\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv Q^{\prime}
.

(c)

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then define
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}:=\{Q^{\ddagger}\mid Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}\wedge P^{\prime}\not\equiv_{Y}Q^{\ddagger}\}
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
is closed under negation and conjunction, there exist a formula
\varphi^{\ddagger}\in\mathbb{L}_{b}^{r}
such that
P^{\prime}\models_{Y}\varphi^{\ddagger}
and, for all
Q^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
Q^{\ddagger}\not\models_{Y}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Note that
P\models_{X}\langle Y\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Thus,
Q\models_{X}\langle Y\rangle\varphi^{\ddagger}
. Therefore, there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\models_{Y}\varphi^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\prime}\equiv_{Y}Q^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix F
Correctness of Time-out Bisimulation

Proof of Proposition
15
.

Let
\R
be a concrete branching reactive bisimulation, let’s define

\displaystyle\B:=\{(P,Q)\mid\R(P,Q)\}\cup\{(\theta_{X}(P),\theta_{X}(Q))\mid\R(P,X,Q)\}

We are going to show that
\B
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
. By definition of
\B
,
\R(P,Q)
or
P=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Thus, by definition of
\B
,
P\B Q_{1}
and
P\B Q_{2}
.

•

if
P=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
then

–

if
\alpha=\tau
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
\R(P^{\ddagger},X,Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
such that, by the definition of
\B
,
P\B\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
P^{\prime}\B\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
.

–

if
\alpha=a\in A
then, by the semantics,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
and
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

*

if
a\in X
then, since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that, by the definition of
\B
,
P\B\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
P^{\prime}\B Q_{2}
.

*

if
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, then there is a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger}_{0})
. Now there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}_{0}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\R(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
. Moreover, we find that
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that, by the definition of
\B
,
P\B\theta_{X}(Q_{1})
and
P^{\prime}\B Q_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then
\R(P,X,Q)
, so there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. By definition of
\B
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\B\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
.

•

if
P=\theta_{Y}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{Y}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},Y,Q^{\dagger})
then, by the semantics of
\theta_{Y}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
. Therefore, using Lemma
2
.4, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q_{1})\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
. By the semantics,there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{Y}(Q_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with, by the definition of
\B
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\B\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
.

3.

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then
\R(P,\emptyset,Q)
, so there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

if
P=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
then, by the semantics,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(Q_{0})\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Let
\B
be a concrete branching time-out bisimulation, let’s define

\displaystyle\R=\{(P,Q)\mid P\B Q\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)\}

We are going to show that
\R
is a generalised concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R(P,Q)
then
P\B Q
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P\B Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\B Q_{2}
, thus, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\B\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. Thus, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(c)

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P\B Q_{0}
, thus, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,Q_{0})
.

2.

If
\R(P,X,Q)
then
\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})
. Therefore, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\theta_{X}(P)\B Q^{\dagger}
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\B Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\dagger}=\theta_{X}(Q_{1})
,
Q^{\ddagger}=\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
. Moreover, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},X,Q_{2})
.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
. Therefore, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
such that
\theta_{X}(P)\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\prime}\B Q_{2}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\dagger}=\theta_{X}(Q_{1})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}
. Moreover, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,X,Q_{1})
and
\R(P^{\prime},Q_{2})
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\mathcal{I}(\theta_{X}(P))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
. Therefore,
\theta_{X}(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{Y}(P^{\prime})\B\theta_{Y}(Q_{2})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\dagger}_{1}=\theta_{X}(Q_{1})
and we have
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
. Moreover, by definition of
\R
, and
\R(P^{\prime},Y,Q_{2})
.

(d)

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Therefore, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(Q)\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
\theta_{X}(P)\B Q^{\dagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\dagger}=\theta_{X}(Q_{0})
and
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Moreover, by definition of
\R
,
\R(P,X,Q_{0})
.

This ends the proof of Proposition
15
.1, and thereby its corollary
15
.2.

Let
\R
be a generalised rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation, let’s define

\displaystyle\B:=\{(P,Q)\mid\R(P,Q)\}\cup\{(\theta_{X}(P),\theta_{X}(Q))\mid\R(P,X,Q)\}

We are going to show that
\B
is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
, by definition of
\B
,
\R(P,Q)
or
P=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P,X,Q)
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

if
P=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
then

–

if
\alpha=\tau
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
. Moreover, by Proposition
15
.
2
,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
.

–

if
\alpha=a\in A
then, by the semantics,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
and
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},X,Q^{\dagger})
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then

•

if
\R(P,Q)
then there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. Thus,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
by Proposition
15
.
2
.

•

if
P=\theta_{Y}(P^{\dagger})
,
Q=\theta_{Y}(Q^{\dagger})
and
\R(P^{\dagger},Y,Q^{\dagger})
then, by the semantics,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\R(P^{\dagger},Y,Q^{\dagger})
,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. Thus,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.

Let
\B
be a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation, let’s define

\displaystyle B:=\{(P,Q)\mid P\B Q\}\cup\{(P,X,Q)\mid\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)\}

We are going to show that
\R
is a generalised rooted concrete branching reactive bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
\R(P,Q)
then
P\B Q
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. Thus,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
, by Proposition
15
.
2
.

2.

If
\R(P,X,Q)
then
\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)
.

(a)

If
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{\tau}\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})
. Since
\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)
, there exists a transition
\theta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
and there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\prime}
. By Proposition
15
.
2
,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
P\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
with
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
. Since
\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)
, there exists a transition
\theta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap((X\cup Y)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\theta_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\theta_{X}(P)\B\theta_{X}(Q)
, there exists a transition
\theta_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{Y}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{Y}(Q^{\prime})
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
. By Proposition
15
.
2
,
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cY}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix G
Congruence Proofs for
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}

To prove congruence properties, the notion of bisimulation
up to
, introduced by Milner in
[
17
]
, is going to be helpful. Let
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,
denote the classical notion of strong bisimilarity
[
17
]
: A
(strong) bisimulation
is a symmetric relation
{\R}\subseteq\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
with
P\mathrel{\R}Q
, if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in Act
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\R}Q^{\prime}
; write
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
if
P\mathrel{\R}Q
for some strong bisimulation
\R
.

Definition 36
.

A
concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}

is a symmetric relation
{\B}\subseteq\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
with
P\B Q
,

1.

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{2}

2.

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})

3.

if
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,

where
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
stands for the relational composition
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\circ\B\circ\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
.

Proposition 37
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
. Then
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
iff there exists a concrete branching time-out bisimulation
\mathcal{B}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
such that
P\B Q
.

Proof.

First of all, a concrete branching time-out bisimulation is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
by reflexivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
. Conversely, let
\B
be a concrete branching bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
. We are going to show that
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation. By the reflexivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
this will suffice. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
. Then there exists
P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
, there exists a transition
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\star}
and
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
Q^{\star}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{1}
and
Q^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{2}
. Since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
is transitive,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there exists a transition
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
such that
Q^{\dagger}_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{2}
. Since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
is transitive and a congruence for
\theta_{X}

[
11
]
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
.

3.

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
Q^{\star}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{0}
.
∎

The following lemma was proven in
[
11
, Appendix B]
. It will be useful in the proof of Proposition
17
.

Lemma 38
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
,
X,S,I\subseteq A
,
\mathcal{R}\subseteq A\times A
.

•

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap X\subseteq S
then
\theta_{X}(P\parallel_{S}Q)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(P\parallel_{S}\theta_{X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P))}(Q))
.

•

\theta_{X}(\tau_{I}(P))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\tau_{I}(\theta_{X\cup I}(P)))
.

•

\theta_{X}(\mathcal{R}(P))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)}(P)))
.

Proof of Proposition
17
.

Let
\B
be the smallest relation satisfying, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
,

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
then
P\B Q

•

if
P\B Q
and
\alpha\in Act
then
\alpha.P\B\alpha.Q

•

if
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
,
P_{2}\B Q_{2}
and
S\subseteq A
then
P_{1}\parallel_{S}P_{2}\B Q_{1}\parallel_{S}Q_{2}

•

if
P\B Q
and
I\subseteq A
then
\tau_{I}(P)\B\tau_{I}(Q)

•

if
P\B Q
and
\mathcal{R}\subseteq A\times A
then
\mathcal{R}(P)\B\mathcal{R}(Q)

•

if
P\B Q
and
L\subseteq U\subseteq A
then
\theta_{L}^{U}(P)\B\theta_{L}^{U}(Q)
.

We are going to show that
\B
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
. This implies that
{\B}={\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}
, using Proposition
37
, and as
\B
is a congruence for the operators of Proposition
17
, so is
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
. Before we do so, we show, by induction on the construction of
\B
, that

if
P\B Q
and
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}
for some
Q^{\prime}
with
P\B Q^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

(1)

Let
P\B Q
and
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
then, by Clause 3 of Definition
13
,
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}
for some
Q^{\prime}
with
Q^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By (the symmetric counterpart of) Clause 1, one obtains
P\B Q^{\prime}
. Clause 1 gives
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

•

If
P=\alpha.P^{\dagger}
and
Q=\alpha.Q^{\dagger}
with
\alpha\in Act
then note that
\alpha\neq\tau
and take
Q^{\prime}:=Q
. One has
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

•

If
P=P_{1}\parallel_{S}P_{1}
and
Q=Q_{1}\parallel_{S}Q_{2}
with
S\subseteq A
and
P_{i}\B Q_{i}
for
i=1,2
, then, for
i=1,2
,
P_{i}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so by induction
Q_{i}\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{i}
for some
Q^{\prime}_{i}
with
P_{i}\B Q^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime}_{i})=\mathcal{I}(P_{i})
.
Now
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{1}\|_{S}Q^{\prime}_{2}
,
P\B Q^{\prime}_{1}\|_{S}Q^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1}\|_{S}Q^{\prime}_{2})=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

•

If
P=\tau_{I}(P_{1})
and
Q=\tau_{I}(Q_{1})
with
I\subseteq A
and
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
, then
P_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so by induction
Q_{1}\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{1}
for some
Q^{\prime}_{1}
with
P_{1}\B Q^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1})=\mathcal{I}(P_{1})
. Now
Q\Longrightarrow\tau_{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
,
P\B\tau_{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
and
\mathcal{I}(\tau_{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1}))=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

•

If
P=\mathcal{R}(P_{1})
and
Q=\mathcal{R}(Q_{1})
with
\mathcal{R}\subseteq A\times A
and
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
, then
P_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so by induction
Q_{1}\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{1}
for some
Q^{\prime}_{1}
with
P_{1}\B Q^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1})=\mathcal{I}(P_{1})
. Now
Q\Longrightarrow\mathcal{R}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
,
P\B\mathcal{R}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
and
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{R}(Q^{\prime}_{1}))=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

•

If
P=\theta_{L}^{U}(P_{1})
and
Q=\theta_{L}^{U}(Q_{1})
with
L\subseteq U\subseteq A
and
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
, then
P_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so by induction
Q_{1}\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{1}
for some
Q^{\prime}_{1}
with
P_{1}\B Q^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime}_{1})=\mathcal{I}(P_{1})
. Now
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
,
P\B\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\prime}_{1})
and
\mathcal{I}(\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\prime}_{1}))=\mathcal{I}(P)
.

We now check that
\B
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
. Note that
\B
is symmetric because
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is.
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
was proven to be a congruence for
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
in
[
11
]
. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then we have to find a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{2}
. Remember that
{\B}\subseteq{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}}
. We are going to proceed by structural induction on
P
and by case distinction on the derivation of
P\B Q
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
then, by definition of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
, thus, by definition of
\B
,
P\B Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\B Q_{2}
.

•

If
P=\beta.P^{\dagger}
and
Q=\beta.Q^{\dagger}
with
\beta\in Act
and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=P^{\dagger}
,
\beta=\alpha
, and thus there exists a path
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\dagger}
such that
P\B Q
and
P^{\prime}\B Q^{\dagger}
.

•

If
P=P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}P^{\ddagger}
and
Q=Q^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
with
S\subseteq A
,
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
then

–

if
\alpha\in S
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
and
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
. Note that
\alpha\neq\tau
because
\alpha\in A
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
, by induction, there exist two paths
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}^{\dagger}
and
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}
,
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
,
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q_{1}^{\ddagger}
and
P^{\prime{\ddagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}
. By the semantics,
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{1}\linebreak\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\dagger}_{2}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}
. Moreover, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}
.

–

if
\alpha\not\in S
then, by the semantics, two cases are possible. Suppose that
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}P^{\ddagger}
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
; the other case is symmetrical. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, by induction, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}
and
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
. Moreover, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
.

If
P=\tau_{I}(P^{\dagger})
and

with

and

then, by the semantics,

,

and
(\beta\in I\wedge\alpha=\tau)\vee\beta=\alpha
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, by induction, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\beta\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}
and
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
. By the semantics,
Q\Longrightarrow\tau_{I}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\tau_{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
such that, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\tau_{I}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})
and

.

and
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
. By the semantics,
Q\Longrightarrow\mathcal{R}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\mathcal{R}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
such that, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\mathcal{R}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})
and

If
P=\theta_{L}^{U}(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger})
with
L\subseteq U\subseteq A
and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
then

–

if
\alpha=\tau
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=\theta_{X}(P^{\prime{\dagger}})
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, by induction, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}
and
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
such that, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
.

–

if
\alpha=a\in A
then, by the semantics,
a\in U\vee\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P^{\prime}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, by induction there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}Q_{2}^{\dagger}
such that
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
. Moreover, in case
a\notin U
we have
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
so (
1
) ensures that
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}
for some
Q^{\prime}
with
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(P)
. This implies that we may choose
Q_{1}^{\dagger}
such that
Q^{\prime}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\dagger}
, and thus
Q^{\prime}=Q_{1}^{\dagger}
. This gives us
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{a}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
such that, by definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then we have to find a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}

with

\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. Remember that
{\B}\subseteq{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}}
. We are going to proceed by structural induction on
P
and by case distinction on the derivation of
P\B Q
.

, there exists a path

If
P=P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}P^{\ddagger}
and
Q=Q^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
with
S\subseteq A
,
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
Q^{\dagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
then, since
{\rm t}\not\in S
, by the semantics, two cases are possible. Suppose that
P^{\prime}=P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
and
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
; the other case is symmetrical. Since
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap X\subseteq S
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\ddagger})\cap((X\setminus S)\cup(X\cap S\cap\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Note that
(X\setminus S)\cup(X\cap S\cap\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))=X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))
. Since
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
and
\mathcal{I}(P^{\ddagger})\cap(X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, by induction, there exists a path
Q^{\ddagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\ddagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}
with
\theta_{X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))}(P^{\prime{\ddagger}})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))}(Q^{\ddagger}_{2r})
. Moreover, by (
1
), since
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}_{0}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{0})=\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{0}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{0}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}
. By Lemma
38
, the definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\mbox{}

\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}\theta_{X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}))}(P^{\prime{\ddagger}}))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{0}\parallel_{S}\theta_{X\setminus(S\setminus\mathcal{I}(Q_{0}^{\dagger}))}(Q^{\ddagger}_{2}))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{0}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}_{2}).

•

If
P=\tau_{I}(P^{\dagger})
and

with

and

with
\theta_{X\cup I}(P^{\prime{\dagger}})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X\cup I}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. Lemma
38
, the definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,

\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\tau_{I}(\theta_{X\cup I}(P^{\prime{\dagger}})))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\tau_{I}(\theta_{X\cup I}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\tau_{I}(Q_{2}^{\dagger})).

•

If
P=\mathcal{R}(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\mathcal{R}(Q^{\dagger})
with

and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
then, by the
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since

Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
with
\theta_{\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)}(P^{\prime{\dagger}})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. By the semantics,
Q\Longrightarrow\mathcal{R}(Q_{1}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\mathcal{R}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. By Lemma
38
, the definition of
\B
and the congruence property of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,

\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)}(P^{\prime{\dagger}})))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)}(Q^{\dagger}_{2r})))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\mathcal{R}(Q_{2r}^{\dagger})).

•

If
P=\theta_{L}^{U}(P^{\dagger})
and
Q=\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger})
with
L\subseteq U\subseteq A
and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
then, by the semantics,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(L\cup X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, (
1
) ensures that
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{0}
for some
Q^{\dagger}_{0}
with
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}_{0}
and
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{0})=\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}_{0}
, by induction, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. As
Q^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
we have
Q^{\dagger}_{0}=Q^{\dagger}_{1}
. As
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})=\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})
one has
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow\theta_{L}^{U}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
.

3.

The last condition of Definition
36
is implied by (
1
).
∎

Appendix H
Full Congruence Proofs for
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}

Definition 39
.

Here, a
rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is a symmetric relation
{\B}\subseteq\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
with
P\B Q
,

1.

if
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}

2.

if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.

Proposition 40
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
. Then
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
iff there exists a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation
\B
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
such that
P\B Q
.

Proof.

First of all, a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
by reflexivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
. Conversely, we are going to show that
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation. This implies that
{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}
, so that each rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is in fact a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
. There exists
P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
, there is a transition
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Thus, by Clause 1 of Definition
39
, there is a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. By Clause 1 of Definition
13
there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
with
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
Q^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By the transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
we obtain
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
for some
P^{\ddagger}
with
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Thus, by Clauses 1 and 2 of Definition
39

\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and there is a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
. By Clause 2 of Definition
13
there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. Since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
is a congruence for
\theta_{X}

[
11
, Theorem 20]
, we have
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
. By the transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
we obtain
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
.

3.

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P^{\dagger}
,
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, so by Clause 1 of Definition
39

Q^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, and by Clause 3 of Definition
13
there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
18
.

Let
{\B}\subseteq\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}
be the smallest relation such that

•

if
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
then
P\B Q

•

if
P\B Q
and
\alpha\in Act
then
\alpha.P\B\alpha.Q

•

if
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
and
P_{2}\B Q_{2}
then
P_{1}+P_{2}\B Q_{1}+Q_{2}

•

if
P_{1}\B Q_{1}
,
P_{2}\B Q_{2}
and
S\subseteq A
then
P_{1}\parallel_{S}P_{2}\B Q_{1}\parallel_{S}Q_{2}

•

if
P\B Q
and
I\subseteq A
then
\tau_{I}(P)\B\tau_{I}(Q)

•

if
P\B Q
and
\mathcal{R}\subseteq A\times A
then
\mathcal{R}(P)\B\mathcal{R}(Q)

•

if
P\B Q
and
L\subseteq U\subseteq A
then
\theta_{L}^{U}(P)\B\theta_{L}^{U}(Q)

•

if
P\B Q
and
X\subseteq A
then
\psi_{X}(P)\B\psi_{X}(Q)

•

if
\mathcal{S}
is a recursive specification with
z\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\rho,\nu\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
are substitutions such that
\forall x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}},\;\rho(x)\B\nu(x)
, then
\langle z|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\B\langle z|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]
.

•

if
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
are recursive specifications and
x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}=V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
with
\langle x|\mathcal{S}\rangle,\langle x|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{P}
such that
\forall y\in V_{\mathcal{S}},\;\mathcal{S}_{y}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{y}
, then
\langle x|\mathcal{S}\rangle\B\langle x|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
.

Note that since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
,
\B
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
are congruences for the operators listed in Proposition
17
, so are the composed relations
\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
. (£)

Let
{=_{\mathcal{I}}}:=\{(P,Q)\mid\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(Q)\}
. A trivial induction on the derivation of
P\B Q
, using the fact that
=_{\mathcal{I}}
is a full congruence for
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}

[
11
]
, shows that

\displaystyle P\B Q\Rightarrow\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(Q)

(
@
)

(For the second last case, the assumption that
\rho(x)\B\nu(x)
for all
x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}
implies
\rho=_{\mathcal{I}}\nu
by induction. Since
=_{\mathcal{I}}
is a lean congruence, this implies
\langle z|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]=_{\mathcal{I}}\langle{z|\mathcal{S}}\rangle[\nu]
.)

A trivial induction on
\mathbb{E}
shows that

\displaystyle\forall E\in\mathbb{E},\rho,\nu\in V\rightarrow\mathbb{P},\;(\forall x\mathbin{\in}V,\rho(x)\B\nu(x))\Rightarrow E[\rho]\B E[\nu]

(
\star
)

A useful corollary is

\displaystyle\forall E\in\mathbb{E},\mathcal{S}\mbox{ a recursive specification},\rho,\nu\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P},

(
\$
)

\displaystyle(\forall x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}},\;\rho(x)\B\nu(x))\Rightarrow\langle E|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\B\langle E|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]

Applied in the context of the last condition of
\B
, it implies

\displaystyle\forall E\in\mathbb{E},\mbox{the variables of {E} are in }V_{\mathcal{S}}\Rightarrow\langle E|\mathcal{S}\rangle\B\langle E|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle

(
\#
)

Since
{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}}\subseteq{\B}
, it suffices to prove that
\B
is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
(so that
{\B}={\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}}
). Note that
\B
is symmetric, since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then we need to find a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. This is sufficient as
{\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}
. We are going to proceed by induction on the proof of
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
and by case distinction on the derivation of
P\B Q
.

•

If
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
then there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
, and so
P^{\prime}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

If
P=\beta.P^{\dagger}
and
Q=\beta.Q^{\dagger}
such that
\beta\in Act
and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
then
\alpha=\beta
and
P^{\prime}=P^{\dagger}
. Thus there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\dagger}
such that
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, and so
P^{\dagger}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\dagger}
.

•

If
P=P^{\dagger}+P^{\ddagger}
and
Q=Q^{\dagger}+Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
then, by the semantics,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
or
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
. Suppose that
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
(the other case proceeds symmetrically). Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

•

If
P=P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}P^{\ddagger}
and
Q=Q^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
S\subseteq A
,
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
then

–

if
\alpha\not\in S
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}P^{\ddagger}
and
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
or
P^{\prime}=P^{\dagger}\parallel_{S}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
and
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
. Suppose that
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
(the other case proceeds symmetrically). Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a transition
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}
such that
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
such that, by (£),
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}Q^{\ddagger}
.

–

if
\alpha\in S
then, by the semantics,
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
,
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\dagger}}
and
P^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime{\ddagger}}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists two transitions
Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}
and
Q^{\ddagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime{\ddagger}}
such that
P^{\prime{\dagger}}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}
and
P^{\prime{\ddagger}}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime{\ddagger}}
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}Q^{\prime{\ddagger}}
such that, by (£),
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime{\dagger}}\parallel_{S}Q^{\prime{\ddagger}}
.

Let

a recursive specification,

and
\rho,\nu\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
such that
\forall x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}},\;\rho(x)\B\nu(x)
. By the semantics,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
is provable by a strict sub-proof of
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
. Moreover, according to
(\$)
,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\B\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]
. By induction, there exists a transition
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}\!
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
\langle z|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

Let

and

and
x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
. By the semantics,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}

(\#)
,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle\B\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
. By induction, there exists a transition
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}
such that

. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}

\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
,
P^{\prime}\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then we need to find a transition

such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. We are going to proceed by induction on the proof of
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
and by case distinction on the derivation of
P\B Q
.

•

such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
and so
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.

Q^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, since
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(Q)
, using (
@
). Now apply that
{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}}
and the transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}
and
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}

\rho,\nu\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
such that
\forall x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}},\rho(x)\B\nu(x)
. By the semantics,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
is provable by a strict sub-proof of
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho])
. Moreover, according to
(\$)
,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]\B\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]
. By induction, there exists a transition
\langle\mathcal{S}_{z}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
\langle z|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.

•

Let
P=\langle x|\mathcal{S}\rangle
and
Q=\langle x|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
with
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
two recursive specifications such that
\forall y\in V_{\mathcal{S}}=V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}},\mathcal{S}_{y}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{y}
and
x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
. By the semantics,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
is provable be a strict sub-proof of
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle)
. Moreover, according
(\#)
,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle\B\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
. By induction, there exists a transition
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
. Since
\langle\_|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\in V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
and
\mathcal{S}_{x}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{x}
,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}^{\prime}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
. Moreover, according to
(@)
,
\mathcal{I}(\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Therefore, there exists a transition
\langle\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{x}|\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. By the semantics, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that, by transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\,\B\,\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.

As a result,
\mathcal{B}
is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
