\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
, and
(\star)
gives us that
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a lean congruence and the last condition of
\B
adds that it is a full congruence.
∎

Appendix I
Proof of RSP

To prove RSP, another version of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is needed, this time up to itself.

Definition 41
.

A
concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is a symmetric relation
{\B}\subseteq\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}
such that, for all
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
, and for all
X\subseteq A
,

1.

if
P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}

2.

if
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})

3.

if
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
then there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Proposition 42
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
. Then
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
iff there exists a concrete branching time-out bisimulation
\mathcal{B}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
such that
P\B Q
.

Proof.

Let
\B
be a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
. We are going to show that
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
. Then there exists
P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\star}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}
and
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
such that
P^{\star}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\star}
and
P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\ddagger}
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\star}
and
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
such that
Q^{\star}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
; moreover, since
Q^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that
Q^{\star}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
Q^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. As a result, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
such that, by transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\dagger}_{2}
with
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}_{1}
,
\mathcal{I}(P_{1}^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger}_{2})
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\dagger}_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger}_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger}_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(Q^{\dagger}_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. As a result, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
.

3.

If
P\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
P^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, and
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}_{0}
. Since
P^{\dagger}\B Q^{\dagger}
, there exists a path
Q^{\dagger}\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
Q^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

The following lemma will be useful to deal with the matching of paths.

Lemma 43
.

Let
H\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{E}
be well-guarded and have free variables from
W\mathbin{\subseteq}V
only, and let
\rho,\nu\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}^{W}\!\!
.

1.

\mathcal{I}(H[\rho])=\mathcal{I}(H[\nu])
.

2.

If
H[\rho]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R
with
\alpha\in Act
then there exists
H^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only such that
R=H^{\prime}[\rho]
and
H[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
.
Moreover, in case
\alpha=\tau
, also
H^{\prime}
is well-guarded.

Proof.

1
. has been proven in
[
11
]
. We obtain
2
. by induction on the derivation of
H[\rho]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R
, making a case distinction on the shape of
H
.

Let
H=\alpha.G
, so that
H[\rho]=\alpha.G[\rho]
. Then
R=G[\rho]
and
H[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}G[\nu]
. In case
\alpha=\tau
, also
G
is well-guarded.

The case
H=0
cannot occur. Nor can the case
H=x\in V
, as
H
is well-guarded.

Let
H=H_{1}\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}
, so that
H[\rho]=H_{1}[\rho]\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}[\rho]
. Note that
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are well-guarded and have free variables in
W
only. One possibility is that
a\notin S
,
H_{1}[\rho]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R_{1}
and
R=R_{1}\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}[\rho]
. By induction,
R_{1}
has the form
H^{\prime}_{1}[\rho]
for some term
H^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only, and in case
\alpha=\tau
, also
H^{\prime}_{1}
is well-guarded. Moreover,
H_{1}[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}_{1}[\nu]
. Thus
R=(H^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2})[\rho]
, and
H^{\prime}:=H^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}
has free variables in
W
only. In case
\alpha=\tau
,
H
is well-guarded. Moreover,
H[\nu]=H_{1}[\nu]\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}_{1}[\nu]\mathbin{\|_{S}}H_{2}[\nu]=H^{\prime}[\nu]
.

The other two cases for
\mathbin{\|_{S}}
, and the cases for the operators
+
and
\mathcal{R}
, are equally trivial.

Let
H=\theta_{L}^{U}(H^{\dagger})
, so that
H[\rho]=\theta_{L}^{U}(H^{\dagger}[\rho])
. Note that
H^{\dagger}
is well-guarded and has free variables in
W
only. The case
\alpha=\tau
is again trivial, so assume
\alpha\neq\tau
. Then
H^{\dagger}[\rho]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R
and either
\alpha\in X
or
\mathcal{I}(H^{\dagger}[\rho])\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By induction,
R
has the form
H^{\prime}[\rho]
for some term
H^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only. Moreover,
H^{\dagger}[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Since
\mathcal{I}(H^{\dagger}[\rho])=\mathcal{I}((H^{\dagger}[\nu])
by Lemma
43
.
1
, either
\alpha\in X
or
\mathcal{I}(H^{\dagger}[\nu])\cap(L\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Consequently,
H[\nu]=\theta_{L}^{U}(H^{\dagger}[\nu])\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
.

Let
H=\psi_{X}(H^{\dagger})
, so that
H[\rho]=\psi_{X}(H^{\dagger}[\rho])
. Note that
H^{\dagger}
is well-guarded and has free variables in
W
only. The case
\alpha\in A\cup\{\tau\}
is trivial, so assume
\alpha={\rm t}
. Then
H^{\dagger}[\rho]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\dagger}
for some
R^{\dagger}
such that
R=\theta_{X}(R^{\dagger})
. Moreover,
H^{\dagger}[\rho]\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By induction,
R^{\dagger}
has the form
H^{\prime}[\rho]
for some term
H^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only. Moreover,
H^{\dagger}[\nu]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Thus
R=(\theta_{X}(H^{\prime}))[\rho]
and
\theta_{X}(H^{\prime})
has free variables in
W
only. Since
\mathcal{I}(H^{\dagger}[\rho])=\mathcal{I}((H^{\dagger}[\nu])
by Lemma
43
.
1
,
H^{\dagger}[\nu]\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Consequently,
H[\nu]=\psi_{X}(H^{\dagger}[\nu])\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\theta_{X}(H^{\prime}[\nu])=(\theta_{X}(H^{\prime}))[\nu]
.

Finally, let
H=\langle x|\mathcal{S}\rangle
, so that
H[\rho]=\langle x|\mathcal{S}[\rho^{\dagger}]\rangle
, where
\rho^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{P}^{W{\setminus}V_{\mathcal{S}}}
is the restriction of
\rho
to
W{\setminus}V_{\mathcal{S}}
. The transition
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}[\rho^{\dagger}]|\mathcal{S}[\rho^{\dagger}]\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}R
is derivable through a subderivation of the one for
\langle x|\mathcal{S}[\rho^{\dagger}]\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}R
. Moreover,
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}[\rho^{\dagger}]|\mathcal{S}[\rho^{\dagger}]\rangle=\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\rho]
. So by induction,
R
has the form
H^{\prime}[\rho]
for some term
H^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only, and
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Moreover, in case
\alpha=\tau
, also
H^{\prime}
is well-guarded. Since
\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]=\langle\mathcal{S}_{x}[\nu^{\dagger}]|\mathcal{S}[\nu^{\dagger}]\rangle
, it follows that
H[\nu]=\langle x|\mathcal{S}\rangle[\nu]=\langle x|\mathcal{S}[\nu^{\dagger}]\rangle\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
.
∎

Corollary 44
.

Let
H\in\mathbb{E}
be well-guarded and have free variables from
W\subseteq V
only, and let
\rho,\nu\in\mathbb{P}^{W}
. If
H[\rho]\Longrightarrow R\xrightarrow{\alpha}S
with
\alpha\in Act
then there exists
H^{\prime},H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables in
W
only such that
R=H^{\prime}[\rho]
,
S=H^{\prime}[\rho]
and
H[\nu]\Longrightarrow H^{\prime}[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
.

Proof of Proposition
21
.

It suffices to prove the proposition when
\rho,\nu\in\mathbb{P}^{V_{\mathcal{S}}}
and only variables of
V_{\mathcal{S}}
can occur in the expressions
\mathcal{S}_{x}
for
x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
. Indeed, the general case requires to prove that, for all
\sigma:V\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
,
\rho[\sigma]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\nu[\sigma]
. Let
\hat{\sigma}:V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
be defined as
\forall x\in V\setminus V_{\mathcal{S}},\hat{\sigma}(x)=\sigma(x)
. Since
\rho\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}[\rho]
,
\rho[\sigma]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}[\rho][\sigma]=\mathcal{S}[\hat{\sigma}][\rho[\sigma]]
, therefore, proving the proposition with
\rho[\sigma]
,
\nu[\sigma]
and
\mathcal{S}[\hat{\sigma}]
is sufficient.

It also suffices to prove the proposition for the case that
\mathcal{S}
is manifestly well-guarded. Indeed, if
\mathcal{S}
is well-guarded, let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be the manifestly well-guarded specification into which
\mathcal{S}
can be converted. Since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a lean congruence, a solution to
\mathcal{S}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a solution to
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a manifestly well-guarded recursive specification with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only, and
\rho,\nu\in\mathbb{P}^{V_{\mathcal{S}}}
two of its solutions up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
. We are going to show that the symmetric closure of

\displaystyle\B:=\{(H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]],H[\mathcal{S}[\nu]])\mid H\in\mathbb{E}\mbox{ is without $\tau_{I}$ and with free variables from }V_{\mathcal{S}}\mbox{ only}\}

is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
. Here
\mathcal{S}[\rho]:=\{x=\mathcal{S}_{x}[\rho]\mid x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}\}\in\mathbb{P}^{V_{\mathcal{S}}}
is employed as a substitution. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
. Then there exists
H\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P=H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
and
Q=H[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
, the other case being symmetrical. Note that
H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]=H[\mathcal{S}][\rho]
. Since
H
and
\mathcal{S}
have free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only, so does
H[\mathcal{S}]
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{S}
is manifestly well-guarded,
H[\mathcal{S}]
is well-guarded.

1.

Let
H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
. By Corollary
44
, there exists
H_{1},H_{2}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P_{1}=H_{1}[\rho]
,
P_{2}=H_{2}[\rho]
and
H[\mathcal{S}][\nu]\Longrightarrow H_{1}[\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H_{2}[\nu]
. Furthermore, since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a congruence and
\rho
and
\nu
are solutions of
\mathcal{S}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
,
H_{1}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{1}[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
,
H_{1}[\nu]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{1}[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
,
H_{2}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{2}[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
and
H_{2}[\nu]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{2}[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
. Therefore, by definition of
\B
,
H_{1}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{1}[\nu]
and
H_{2}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{2}[\nu]
.

2.

Let
H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By Corollary
44
, there exists
H_{1},H_{2}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P_{1}=H_{1}[\rho]
,
P_{2}=H_{2}[\rho]
and
H[\mathcal{S}][\nu]\Longrightarrow H_{1}[\nu]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}H_{2}[\nu]
. Since
H_{1}
is well-guarded, by Lemma
43
,
\mathcal{I}(H_{1}[\nu])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Furthermore, since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a congruence and
\rho
and
\nu
are solutions of
\mathcal{S}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
,
H_{2}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{2}[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
and
H_{2}[\nu]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H_{2}[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
; therefore, by definition of
\B
,
\theta_{X}(H_{2}[\rho])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(H_{2}[\nu])
(notice that
\theta_{X}(H_{2}[\mathcal{S}][\rho])=\theta_{X}(H_{2})[\mathcal{S}][\rho]
).

3.

Let
H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By Lemma
43
, there exists a well-guarded
H_{1}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P_{0}=H_{0}[\rho]
and
H[\mathcal{S}][\nu]\Longrightarrow H_{0}[\nu]
. Since
H_{0}
is well-guarded and
P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
, according to Lemma
43
.1,
H_{0}[\nu]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Next, we will prove that
\B
is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
. Then there exists
H\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P=H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
and
Q=H[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
, the other case being symmetrical. Note that
H[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]=H[\mathcal{S}][\rho]
. Since
H
and
\mathcal{S}
have free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only, so does
H[\mathcal{S}]
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{S}
is manifestly well-guarded,
H[\mathcal{S}]
is well-guarded.

1.

Let
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
. By Lemma
43
, there exists
H^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P^{\prime}=H^{\prime}[\rho]
and
Q=H[\mathcal{S}][\nu]\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Furthermore, since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a congruence and
\rho
and
\nu
are solutions of
\mathcal{S}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\,
,
H^{\prime}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H^{\prime}[\mathcal{S}[\rho]]
and
H^{\prime}[\nu]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H^{\prime}[\mathcal{S}[\nu]]
. Therefore, by definition of
\B
,
H^{\prime}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\B\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. But,
\B
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\,
, thus, by Proposition
42
,
H^{\prime}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H^{\prime}[\nu]
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
. By Lemma
43
, there exists
H^{\prime}\in\mathbb{E}
with free variables from
V_{\mathcal{S}}
only such that
P^{\prime}=H^{\prime}[\rho]
and
Q=H[\mathcal{S}][\nu]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Exactly as above, not even using
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, this implies
H^{\prime}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}H^{\prime}[\nu]
. Thus, since
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}
is a congruence,
\theta_{X}(H^{\prime}[\rho])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(H^{\prime}[\nu])
.

By considering
H=x
with
x\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
, this yields
\mathcal{S}_{x}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}_{x}[\nu]
and so
\rho(x)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}_{x}[\rho]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\mathcal{S}_{x}[\nu]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\nu(x)
. Consequently,
\rho\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\nu
.
∎

Appendix J
Soundness of the Reactive Approximation Axiom

Lemma 45
.

\forall P\in\mathbb{P},\theta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\theta_{X}(P))

Proof.

Trivial when considering the semantics of
\theta_{X}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
22
.

We show that
\B:=\{(P,Q),(Q,P)\mid\forall X\subseteq A,\;\psi_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\psi_{X}(Q)\}
is a rooted concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
such that
P\B Q
. Thus,
\forall X\subseteq A,\psi_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\psi_{X}(Q)
.

1.

If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then, by the semantics,
\psi_{A}(P)\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
. Since
\psi_{A}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\psi_{A}(Q)
, there exists a transition
\psi_{A}(Q)\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. By the semantics,
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then, by the semantics,
\psi_{X}(P)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})
. Since
\psi_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}\psi_{X}(Q)
, there exists a transition
\psi_{X}(Q)\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
\theta_{X}(\theta_{X}(P^{\prime}))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
. By the semantics,
Q^{\ddagger}=\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
and
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
. By Lemma
45
,
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(\theta_{X}(P^{\prime}))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime}))\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
.
∎

Appendix K
Proofs of Completeness for Finite Processes

Definition 46
.

Call a process
P

\tau
-stable
if, for all transitions
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\dagger}
,
P\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}
.

Lemma 47
.

If
P
and
Q
are
\tau
-stable and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
then
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
.

Proof.

Assume that
P
and
Q
are
\tau
-stable and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
. If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
, then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q_{2}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{2}
. By symmetry and transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
we have
Q_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
, so by the
\tau
-stability of
Q
it follows that
Q_{1}=Q
. Moreover, if
\alpha=\tau
and
Q_{2}=Q_{1}
then
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P
, contradicting the
\tau
-stability of
P
. Thus
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}
. This argument also yields that
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(Q)
.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{2})
. By Lemma
2
.1, Lemma
2
.3 and the transitivity of
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
, we have
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
and
Q\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{1}
, respectively, so the
\tau
-stability of
Q
yields
Q_{1}=Q
. Hence
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{2}
. So indeed
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
25
.

We define the
length
of a path
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{2}}...\xrightarrow{\alpha_{n}}P_{n}
to be
n
and the
depth
of a process
P
, denoted
d(P)
, to be the length of the longest path starting from
P
. It is well defined because
P
is a recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
process. Note that
d(\theta_{X}(P))\leq d(P)
.

(\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!})
We will proceed by induction on
\max(d(P),d(Q))
. Let
n\mathbin{\in}{\rm Nature}
and suppose that the property holds for any recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes
P,Q
such that
\max(d(P),d(Q))<n
. Let
P,Q
be two recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes such that
\max(d(P),d(Q))=n
and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
.

Since
P
is recursion-free, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
and
P_{0}
is
\tau
-stable. We are going to show that, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}=\alpha.\hat{P}_{0}
. If
P
is
\tau
-stable then
P=P_{0}
and this is trivial. Thus, suppose that
P
is not
\tau
-stable, i.e., there exists
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}
. Then, as
P_{0}
is
\tau
-stable,
P\neq P_{0}
and so
d(P_{0})<d(P)
.

Let
I:=\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\wedge(\alpha\neq\tau\vee P\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime})\}
, listing the outgoing transitions of
P
not labelled by
{\rm t}
and not elidable w.r.t.
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\,
. Let
(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
, there exists a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}
. Since
P_{0}
is
\tau
-stable and
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
,
P_{0}=P_{1}
and
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
. If
\alpha=\tau
then
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}
so
P_{0}\neq P_{2}
and
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
. Since
\max(d(P_{2}),d(P^{\prime}))<d(P)
, by induction,
Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}^{\prime}=\alpha.\hat{P}_{2}
, so by Lemma
24

Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.P^{\prime}=\alpha.P_{2}
. As a result,
Ax_{r}\vdash\hat{P}_{0}=\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P^{\prime}}+\hat{P}_{0}
.

Let
J:=\{(\tau,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}\wedge P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}\}
, listing the outgoing
\tau
-transitions of
P
elidable w.r.t.
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\,
. So
J\neq\emptyset
. Let
(\tau,P^{\prime})\in J
. Since
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
and
\max(d(P^{\prime}),d(P_{0}))<d(P)
, by induction,
Ax_{r}\vdash\tau.\hat{P^{\prime}}=\tau.\hat{P_{0}}
, so by Lemma
24

Ax_{r}\vdash\tau.P^{\prime}=\tau.\hat{P_{0}}
.

Now, using
L
τ
, the following equality can be derived from
Ax_{r}
, for all
\beta\in Act
.

\displaystyle Ax_{r}\vdash\beta.\hat{P}=

\displaystyle~\beta.(\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\alpha.{P^{\prime}})=\beta.(\sum_{(\tau,P^{\prime})\in J}\tau.{P}^{\prime}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\beta.(\tau.\hat{P}_{0}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P^{\prime}})=\beta.(\tau.(\hat{P}_{0}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P^{\prime}})+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\beta.(\hat{P}_{0}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.{P^{\prime}})=\beta.\hat{P}_{0}

Likewise, since
Q
is recursion-free, a similar
\tau
-stable
Q_{0}
can be defined. By the same reasoning, it can be proved that, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.\hat{Q}=\alpha.\hat{Q}_{0}
. Since
P_{0}
and
Q_{0}
are
\tau
-stable and
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
,
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
according to Lemma
47
.

To end the proof, it suffices to show that, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}_{0}=\alpha.\hat{Q}_{0}
, but we are going to prove the stronger statement
Ax_{r}\vdash\hat{P}_{0}=\hat{Q}_{0}
. Using the reactive approximation axiom, it suffices to prove that, for all
X\subseteq A
,
Ax_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(P_{0})=\psi_{X}(Q_{0})
.

Let
(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\in\{(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\mid P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}
. Since
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
, there exists a transition
Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that
P_{0}^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q_{0}^{\prime}
. By induction,
Ax_{r}\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}_{0}^{\prime}=\alpha.\hat{Q}_{0}^{\prime}
.

Let
X\subseteq A
and
({\rm t},P_{0}^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{0}^{\prime}
. Since
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q_{0}
, there exists a transition
Q_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{0}^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q_{0}^{\prime})
. By induction,
Ax_{r}\vdash{\rm t}.\widehat{\theta_{X}(P_{0}^{\prime})}={\rm t}.\widehat{\theta_{X}(Q_{0}^{\prime})}
.

Let
X\subseteq A
. If
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})\neq\emptyset
then
\mathcal{I}(Q_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})\neq\emptyset
and, using Lemma
24
,

\displaystyle Ax_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(Q_{0})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.Q_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.Q_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\in\{(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\mid P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.P_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\psi_{X}(P_{0}+Q_{0})

If
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
\mathcal{I}(Q_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and

\displaystyle Ax_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(Q_{0})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.Q_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{\{(t,Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{0}^{\prime}\}}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(Q_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.Q_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\in\{(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})\mid P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{0}^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.P_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\{({\rm t},Q_{0}^{\prime})\mid Q_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q_{0}^{\prime}\}}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(Q_{0}^{\prime})+\sum_{\{({\rm t},P_{0}^{\prime})\mid P_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{0}^{\prime}\}}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(P_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\psi_{X}(P_{0}+Q_{0})

As a result, for all
X\subseteq A
,
Ax_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(Q_{0})=\psi_{X}(P_{0}+Q_{0})
, and so,

Ax_{r}\vdash Q_{0}=P_{0}+Q_{0}
. Symmetrically,
Ax_{r}\vdash P_{0}=P_{0}+Q_{0}
. Therefore,
Ax_{r}\vdash P_{0}=Q_{0}
.

(\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!})
We will proceed by induction on
\max(d(P),d(Q))
. Let
n\mathbin{\in}{\rm Nature}
and suppose that the property holds for any recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes
P,Q
such that
\max(d(P),d(Q))<n
. Let
P,Q
be two recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes such that
\max(d(P),d(Q))=n
and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q
.

Since
P
is recursion-free, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{0}
and, for all
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
,
P_{0}\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}
. We are going to show that, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}=\alpha.\hat{P_{0}}
. If for all
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
,
P\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}
then
P=P_{0}
and it is trivial. Thus, suppose there exists
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}
. Then
P_{0}\neq P
and
d(P_{0})<d(P)
.

Let
J:=\{(\tau,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}\wedge P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}\}
, listing the outgoing
\tau
-transitions of
P
that can be elided w.r.t.
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\,
. Let
(\tau,P^{\prime})\in J
. Since
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{0}
and
\max(d(P^{\prime}),d(P_{0}))<d(P)
, by induction,
Ax\vdash\tau.\hat{P^{\prime}}=\tau.\hat{P}_{0}
.

Let
I:=\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\}\setminus J
, listing the outgoing transitions of
P
that cannot be elided. Let
(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{0}
, there exists a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
such that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{1}
and
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{2}
. Thus,
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{0}
, but, for all
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\dagger}
,
P_{0}\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\dagger}
, so
P_{0}=P_{1}
and
P\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}
. Since
(\alpha,P^{\prime})\not\in J
,
\alpha\in A
or
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P\not\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P_{2}
so
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
and
\max(d(P_{2}),d(P^{\prime}))<d(P)
. Thus, by induction,
Ax\vdash\alpha.\hat{P^{\prime}}=\alpha.\hat{P}_{2}
. As a result,
Ax\vdash\hat{P}_{0}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.\hat{P^{\prime}}=\hat{P}_{0}
.

Since there exists
P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\prime}
,
J\neq\emptyset
.

\displaystyle Ax\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}=

\displaystyle~\alpha.(\sum_{(\tau,P^{\prime})\in J}\tau.\hat{P}^{\prime}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.\hat{P}^{\prime})=\alpha.(\tau.\hat{P}_{0}+\!\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\!\alpha.\hat{P}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\alpha.(\tau.(\hat{P}_{0}+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.\hat{P}^{\prime})+\sum_{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\in I}\alpha.\hat{P}^{\prime})=\alpha.\hat{P}_{0}

Similarly, since
Q
is recursion-free, there exists a recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
process
Q_{0}
such that
Q\Longrightarrow Q_{0}
,
Q\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q_{0}
and, for all
Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\dagger}
,
\neg(Q_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\dagger})
. Moreover, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax\vdash\alpha.\hat{Q}=\alpha.\hat{Q}_{0}
. Notice that
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q_{0}
and, since, for all
Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\tau}Q^{\dagger}
,
\neg(Q_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\dagger})
and, for all
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\dagger}
,
\neg(P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}P^{\dagger})
,
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}Q_{0}
.

Let
(\alpha,P_{0}^{\prime})
such that
P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{0}^{\prime}
. Since
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}Q_{0}
, there exists a path
Q_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{2}
such that
P_{0}^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q_{2}
. Since
\max(d(P_{0}^{\prime}),d(Q_{2}))<n
, by induction,
Ax\vdash\alpha.P_{0}^{\prime}=\alpha.Q_{2}
. As a result,
Ax\vdash\hat{P}_{0}+\hat{Q}_{0}=\hat{Q}_{0}
. Symmetrically,
Ax\vdash\hat{P}_{0}+\hat{Q}_{0}=\hat{P}_{0}
, and so,
Ax\vdash\hat{P}_{0}=\hat{Q}_{0}
. Finally, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax\vdash\alpha.\hat{P}=\alpha.\hat{Q}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
26
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}
be two recursion-free
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes. Let
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
.
P
and
Q
can be equated in the same manner as
P_{0}
and
Q_{0}
in the proof of Proposition
25
.

Suppose that
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}Q
. Let
(\alpha,P^{\prime})
such that
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in Act
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}Q
, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}Q^{\prime}
. According to the previous proposition,
Ax\vdash\alpha.P^{\prime}=\alpha.Q^{\prime}
, thus,

\displaystyle Ax\vdash Q=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\}}\alpha.Q^{\prime}=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\}}\alpha.Q^{\prime}+\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\}}\alpha.P^{\prime}=Q+P

As a result,
Ax\vdash Q=P+Q
. Symmetrically,
Ax\vdash P=P+Q
. Therefore,
Ax\vdash P=Q
.
∎

Appendix L
Proof of Completeness by Equation Merging

Proof of Theorem
27
.

Let
E_{0}
and
F_{0}
two strongly guarded
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes such that
E_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}F_{0}
. We are going to build a recursive specification
\mathcal{S}
such that
E_{0}
and
F_{0}
will be components of solutions of
\mathcal{S}
in the same variable. Let
\mathcal{E}_{E_{0}}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{F_{0}}
) be the set of reachable expressions from
E_{0}
(resp.
F_{0}
). Let
V_{\mathcal{S}}
be a set of fresh variables
\{x_{EF}\mid(E,F)\in\mathcal{E}_{E_{0}}\times\mathcal{E}_{F_{0}}\wedge E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F\}
. We denote
x_{0}=x_{E_{0}F_{0}}\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
and we define the following set of equations
\mathcal{S}
, for all
x_{EF}\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
, with
\alpha\in A\cup\{\tau,{\rm t}\}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{x_{EF}}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime},F\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\hskip-8.0pt\alpha.x_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\tau}E^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F,x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.x_{E^{\prime}F}+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime},x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.x_{EF^{\prime}}

Note that
\mathcal{S}
is well-guarded since
E_{0}
and
F_{0}
are strongly guarded
\mbox{CCSP}^{\theta}_{t}
processes. For
x_{EF}\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
, we define
H_{EF},G_{EF}\in\mathbb{E}
such that

\displaystyle H_{EF}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime},F\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\hskip-8.0pt\alpha.E^{\prime}+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\tau}E^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F,x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.E^{\prime}

\displaystyle G_{EF}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}H_{EF}+\tau.E&\mbox{if }x_{0}\mathbin{\neq}x_{EF}\mbox{, }\exists F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}\\
E&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

According to Lemma
24
, for all
(E,F)\in\mathcal{E}_{E_{0}}\times\mathcal{E}_{F_{0}}
,
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E+H_{EF}=\widehat{(E+H_{EF})}=\hat{E}=E
. Let
(E,F)\in\mathcal{E}_{E_{0}}\times\mathcal{E}_{F_{0}}
. If
x_{0}\neq x_{EF}
and
\exists F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}
then, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax^{\infty}\vdash\alpha.G_{EF}=\alpha.(H_{EF}+\tau.E)=\alpha.E
using the branching axiom. In any case, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax^{\infty}\vdash\alpha.G_{EF}=\alpha.E
.
(*)

If we prove that the family
(G_{EF})_{(E,F)\in\mathcal{E}_{E_{0}}\times\mathcal{E}_{F_{0}}}
is a solution of
\mathcal{S}
then, by definition of
G_{E_{0}F_{0}}
, there would exist a solution whose value for the variable
x_{0}
is
E
. According to
(*)
, we need to prove that, for all
x_{EF}\in V_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}\vdash G_{EF}=

\displaystyle\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime},F\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\hskip-8.0pt\alpha.G_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\tau}E^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F,x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.G_{E^{\prime}F}+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime},x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.G_{EF^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime},F\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\hskip-8.0pt\alpha.E^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{E\xrightarrow{\tau}E^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F,x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.E^{\prime}+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime},x_{EF}\neq x_{0}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.E

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~H_{EF}+\hskip-8.0pt\sum_{x_{EF}\neq x_{0},F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\hskip-8.0pt\tau.E

•

If
x_{EF}\neq x_{0}
and
\exists F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}
then this follows from the definition of
G_{EF}
.

•

If
x_{EF}\neq x_{0}
and
\forall F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},E\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}
then, by definition of
G_{EF}
, we have to prove
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E=H_{EF}
. Let
(\alpha,E^{\prime})
such that
E\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in Act
. Since
E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F
, there exists a path
F\Longrightarrow F_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}F_{2}
such that
E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F_{1}
and
E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F_{2}
. Since
\forall F\xrightarrow{\tau}F^{\prime},\neg(E\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime})
,
F=F_{1}
, so there exists a transition
F\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}F_{2}
such that either
F\xrightarrow{\alpha}F_{2}
and
E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F_{2}
, or
\alpha=\tau
and
E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F
. In either case,
H_{EF}\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime}
.

As a result,
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E=\hat{E}=\widehat{E+H}_{EF}=\hat{H}_{EF}=H_{EF}
.

•

If
x_{EF}=x_{0}
then
E=E_{0}
,
F=F_{0}
and we have to show that
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E_{0}=H_{E_{0}F_{0}}=\sum_{E_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime},F_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime},E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}}\alpha.E^{\prime}
. Let
(\alpha,E^{\prime})
such that
E_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}E^{\prime}
. Since
E_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}F_{0}
, there exists a transition
F_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}F^{\prime}
such that
E^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}F^{\prime}
.
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E=\hat{E}=\hat{H}_{EF}=H_{EF}
.
∎

Note that we could define
H^{\prime}_{EF}
and
G^{\prime}_{EF}
by reverting the role of
E
and
F
and also get a solution whose value for the variable
x_{0}
is
F_{0}
. Consequently, RSP yields
Ax^{\infty}\vdash E_{0}=F_{0}
.

Appendix M
The Canonical Representative

We are going to start by proving some lemmas facilitating the handling of classes.

Lemma 48
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
.

1.

\forall\alpha\in A_{\tau},([P]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\exists P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2},(P_{1},P_{2})\in[P]\times R^{\prime}\wedge(\alpha\in A\vee[P]\neq R^{\prime}))
.

2.

{[P]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}}\Leftrightarrow\exists P_{0}\in[P],P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

3.

Let
X\subseteq A
. Then
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Leftrightarrow\exists P\Longrightarrow P_{0},P_{0}\in[P]\wedge\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

4.

If
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
\exists P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
,
P_{1}\in[P]\wedge\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\wedge\theta_{X}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
.

5.

If
\exists X\mathbin{\subseteq}A,\,\exists P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2},P_{1}\in[P]\wedge\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then there exists an
R^{\prime}
with
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}\wedge\theta_{X}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
.

Proof.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
.

1.

Let
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
.

•

If
[P]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}
then, by definition of
\rightarrow
, there exists a path
\chi([P])\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
,
P_{2}\in R^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in A\vee[P]\neq R^{\prime}
. Since
\chi([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P
, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}^{\prime}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}^{\prime}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}^{\prime}
, thus,
P_{1}^{\prime}\in[P]
and
P_{2}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
. If
\alpha=\tau
then
[P]\neq R^{\prime}
, so
P_{1}^{\prime}\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}^{\prime}
and so
P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
, otherwise,
P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}^{\prime}
.

•

If there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
,
P_{2}\in R^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in A\vee[P]\neq R^{\prime}
then, since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\chi([P])
, there exists a path
\chi([P])\Longrightarrow P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P_{2}^{\prime}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}^{\prime}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}^{\prime}
, thus,
P^{\prime}_{1}\in[P]
and
P^{\prime}_{2}\in R^{\prime}
. If
\alpha=\tau
then
[P]\neq R^{\prime}
, therefore,
P_{1}^{\prime}\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}^{\prime}
and so
P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}^{\prime}
, otherwise,
P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}^{\prime}
. By definition of
\rightarrow
,
[P]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}
.

2.

•

If
[P]\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, according to the previous point, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
and
P_{2}\not\in[P]
. Suppose that there is a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
P_{0}\in[P]
. Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P
, so there exists a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\dagger}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\ddagger}
. Since
P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
P_{0}=P^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}\not\in[P]
, but that’s impossible.

•

Suppose that, for all paths
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
P_{0}\not\in[P]
. Since
P
is strongly guarded, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
and, for all
P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime}
,
P_{1}\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}
. Since
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}
and
P_{1}\in[P]
, there exists a transition
P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P_{2}
, and
P_{1}\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{2}
. Thus, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
and
P_{2}\not\in[P]
. According to the previous point,
[P]\xrightarrow{\tau}[P_{2}]
.

3.

This a corollary of the two previous points.

4.

Suppose that
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, then, by definition of
\rightarrow
, there exists a path
\chi([P])\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}_{2}
such that
P^{\prime}_{1}\in[P]
,
P^{\prime}_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
and
P^{\prime}_{2}\in R^{\prime}
. Since
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}
such that
P_{0}\in[P]
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, thus, since
P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\prime}_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
P^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P
and
P^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}_{2}
, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P_{2}^{\prime})
. As a result, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
,
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{X}(P_{2}^{\prime})]=[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
.

5.

Suppose there exists
X\subseteq A
and a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\chi([P])
, there exists a path
\chi([P])\Longrightarrow P_{1}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}^{\prime}
such that
P_{1}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\prime}_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P^{\prime}_{2})
and so
P_{1}^{\prime}\in[P]
. Therefore, by definition of
\rightarrow
,
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}[P_{2}^{\prime}]
and
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi([P^{\prime}_{2}]))]
.
∎

Corollary 49
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
and
X\subseteq A
.

1.

If
[P]\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
\exists P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}R^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{I}(P^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

2.

If
\theta_{X}(P)\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R))]
,
R\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
\exists P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{I}(P^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The first statement follows directly from Lemma
48
.1–3. For the second, suppose
\theta_{X}(P)\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R))]
,
R\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the first statement,
\chi(R)\Longrightarrow Q^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
for some
Q^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By the semantics of
\theta_{X}
, there is a path
\theta_{X}(\chi(R))\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Since
\theta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(\chi(R))
, there is a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
P^{\dagger}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. By the semantics,
P^{\dagger}=\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})
for some
P^{\prime}
with
P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. So
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
by Proposition
15
.2, and Lemma
2
.3 yields
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. Thus
P^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
and Lemma
2
.2 gives
\mathcal{I}(P^{\prime})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
.
∎

Remark 50
.

Let
R\in[\mathbb{P}^{g}]
. If
R\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}
then
R\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Proof.

Suppose
R\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
. By the definition in Section
5.4
, there is a path
\chi(R)\Longrightarrow P_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
P_{1}\in R
. So by Lemma
48
.2
R\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Definition 51
.

A
concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to reflexivity and transitivity
is a symmetric relation
{\B}
on
\mathbb{P}^{g}\uplus[\mathbb{P}^{g}]\uplus\{\theta_{X}([P])\mathbin{|}X\mathbin{\subseteq}A\wedge P\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}^{g}\}
, such that, for all
P^{\dagger}\!\B Q
,

•

if
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\ddagger}
with
\alpha\mathbin{\in}A_{\tau}
, then
\exists
path
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
P^{\dagger}\B^{*}Q^{\dagger}
and
P^{\ddagger}\B^{*}Q^{\dagger}
,

•

if
P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}
with
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\ddagger}
with
\mathcal{I}(Q^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})\B^{*}\theta_{X}(Q^{\ddagger})
,

•

if
P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then there is a path
Q\Longrightarrow Q^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

Here
\B^{*}:=\{(P^{\dagger},Q^{\dagger})\mid\exists n\geq 0.~\exists P_{0},\dots,P_{n}.~P^{\dagger}=P_{0}\B P_{1}\B\dots\B P_{n}=Q^{\dagger}\}
.

Proposition 52
.

If
P\B Q
for a concrete branching time-out bisimulation
\B
up to reflexivity and transitivity, then
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\B^{*}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation. Clearly this relation is symmetric.

•

Suppose
P_{0}\B P_{1}\B\dots\B P_{n}
for some
n\geq 0
and
P\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}_{0}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
. It suffices to find
P^{\dagger}_{n},P^{\ddagger}_{n}
such that
P_{n}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{n}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
,
P^{\dagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\dagger}_{n}
and
P^{\ddagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
. (In fact, we need this only in the special case where
P_{0}=P^{\dagger}_{0}\neq P^{\ddagger}_{0}
, but establish the more general claim.) We proceed with induction on
n
. The case
n=0
is trivial.

Fixing an
n>0
, by Definition
51
there are
P^{\dagger}_{1},P^{\ddagger}_{1}
such that
P_{1}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}_{1}
,
P^{\dagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\dagger}_{1}
and
P^{\ddagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\ddagger}_{1}
. Now by induction there are
P^{\dagger}_{n},P^{\ddagger}_{n}
such that
P_{n}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{n}\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
,
P^{\dagger}_{1}\B^{*}P^{\dagger}_{n}
and
P^{\ddagger}_{1}\B^{*}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
. Hence
P^{\dagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\dagger}_{n}
and
P^{\ddagger}_{0}\B^{*}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
.

•

Suppose
P_{0}\B P_{1}\B\dots\B P_{n}
for some
n\geq 0
and there is a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{0}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}_{0}
with
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. It suffices to find
P^{\dagger}_{n},P^{\ddagger}_{n}
such that
P_{n}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}_{n})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{0})\B^{*}\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{n})
. (In fact, we need this only in the special case where
P^{\dagger}_{0}=P_{0}
, but establish the more general claim.) We proceed with induction on
n
. The case
n=0
is trivial.

Fixing an
n>0
, by Definition
51
there exist
P^{\dagger}_{1},P^{\ddagger}_{1}
such that
P_{1}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\ddagger}_{1}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{0})\B^{*}\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{1})
. By induction there are
P^{\dagger}_{n},P^{\ddagger}_{n}
with
P_{n}\mathbin{\Longrightarrow}P^{\dagger}_{n}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}}P^{\ddagger}_{n}
,
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger}_{n})\!\cap\!(X\!\cup\!\{\tau\})\mathbin{=}\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{1})\B^{*}\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{n})
. Hence
\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{0})\B^{*}\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger}_{n})
.

•

Suppose
P_{0}\B P_{1}\B\dots\B P_{n}
for some
n\geq 0
and there is a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. It suffices to find a path
P_{n}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{n}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. (In fact, we need this only in the special case where
P^{\dagger}_{0}=P_{0}
, but establish the more general claim.) We proceed with induction on
n
. The case
n=0
is trivial.

Fixing an
n>0
, by Definition
51
there exists a path
P_{1}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{1}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. By induction, there exists a path
P_{n}\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}_{n}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Lemma 53
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
and
X\subseteq A
. If
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
then
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}([P])
.

Proof.

It suffices to see that
\B:=\{([P],\theta_{X}([P])),(\theta_{X}([P]),[P])\}\cup Id
is a strong bisimulation thanks to the semantics of
\theta_{X}
.
∎

Lemma 54
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
and
X\subseteq A
. Then
\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}[\theta_{X}(P)]
.

Proof.

We will show that
{\B}:={{\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}}\cup\!\{(\theta_{X}([P]),[\theta_{X}(\!P)]),([\theta_{X}(\!P)],\theta_{X}([P]))\!\mid\!P\mathbin{\in}\mathbb{P}^{g}\!\wedge X\mathbin{\subseteq}A\}}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to reflexivity and transitivity.

•

If
\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{\tau}R^{\prime}
then
[P]\xrightarrow{\tau}R^{\dagger}
with
R^{\prime}=\theta_{X}(R^{\dagger})
. According to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P]
and
P_{2}\in R^{\dagger}=[P_{2}]
and
[P]\neq R^{\dagger}
. Thus, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(P_{1})\xrightarrow{\tau}\theta_{X}(P_{2})
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{1})\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
. If
\theta_{X}(P_{1})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P_{2})
then
[\theta_{X}(P)]=[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]
. Otherwise,
[\theta_{X}(P)]\neq[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]
, thus, according to Lemma
48
.1,
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{\tau}[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]
. In either case, there exists a transition
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\tau\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]
such that, by definition of
\B
,
[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]\B\theta_{X}([P_{2}])=R^{\prime}
.

•

If
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{\tau}R^{\prime}
then, according to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
,
P_{2}\in R^{\prime}
and
[\theta_{X}(P)]\neq R^{\prime}
. Thus, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\ddagger}
such that
P_{1}=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
and
P_{2}=\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})
. Notice that, since
[P_{1}]\neq[P_{2}]
,
[P^{\dagger}]\neq[P^{\ddagger}]
. According to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
[P]\Longrightarrow[P^{\dagger}]\xrightarrow{\tau}[P^{\ddagger}]
. Thus,
\theta_{X}([P])\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}([P^{\dagger}])\xrightarrow{\tau}\theta_{X}([P^{\ddagger}])
. Moreover, by definition of
\B
,
\theta_{X}([P^{\dagger}])\B[\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})]=[\theta_{X}(P)]
and
\theta_{X}([P^{\ddagger}])\B[\theta_{X}(P^{\ddagger})]=R^{\prime}
.

•

If
\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{a}R^{\prime}
with
a\in A
then
[P]\xrightarrow{a}R^{\prime}
and
a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. If
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, according to Lemma
48
.3, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}
such that
P_{0}\in[P]
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Otherwise, set
P_{0}:=P
. Since
[P_{0}]\xrightarrow{a}R^{\prime}
, according to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
P_{0}\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{a}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[P_{0}]
and
P_{2}\in R^{\prime}
. Notice that
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Rightarrow\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\wedge P_{0}=P_{1}
. Thus, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P_{0})\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(P_{1})\xrightarrow{a}P_{2}
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{1})\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
. According to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a transition
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{a}R^{\prime}
.

•

If
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{a}R^{\prime}
with
a\in A
then, according to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{a}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
and
P_{2}\in R^{\prime}
. Thus, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{a}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
and
