a\in X\vee\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. According to Lemma
48
.1, there exists a path
[P]\Longrightarrow[P^{\dagger}]\xrightarrow{a}[P_{2}]
. Thus,
\theta_{X}([P])\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}([P^{\dagger}])\xrightarrow{a}[P_{2}]
since
\mathcal{I}(P^{\dagger})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset\Rightarrow\mathcal{I}([P^{\dagger}])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
by Lemma
48
.3. Moreover, by definition of
\B
,
\theta_{X}([P^{\dagger}])\B[\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})]=[\theta_{X}(P)]
and
[P_{2}]=R^{\prime}\B^{*}R^{\prime}
.

•

If
\mathcal{I}(\theta_{X}([P]))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
then
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
; thus, according to Lemma
48
.3, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}
such that
P_{0}\in[P]
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Since
\mathcal{I}(P_{0})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}\theta_{X}(P_{0})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P)
and so
[P]=[\theta_{X}(P)]
. Since
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, according to Lemma
53
,
\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}[P]=[\theta_{X}(P)]
.

•

If
\mathcal{I}([\theta_{X}(P)])\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
[\theta_{X}(P)]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
then, according to Lemma
48
.4, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(P_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{1})\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
,
\mathcal{I}(\theta_{X}(P_{1}))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{Y}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{Y}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
. Since
\theta_{X}(P_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
,
P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
and
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, thus,
\theta_{X}(P)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(P_{1})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}P_{1}
. Therefore, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
such that
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. According to Lemma
48
.1 and
48
.5, there exists a path
[P]\Longrightarrow[P_{1}]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime\prime}
for some
R^{\prime\prime}\in[\mathbb{P}^{g}]
with
\theta_{Y}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{Y}(\chi(R^{\prime\prime}))]
. Thus,
\theta_{X}([P])\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}([P_{1}])\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime\prime}
since
\mathcal{I}([P_{1}])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Moreover,
\mathcal{I}(\theta_{X}([P_{1}]))\cap(Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
since
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup Y\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{Y}(R^{\prime})\B[\theta_{Y}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]=[\theta_{Y}(P_{2})]=[\theta_{Y}(\chi(R^{\prime\prime}))]\B\theta_{Y}(R^{\prime\prime})
.

•

If
\theta_{X}([P])\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then
[P]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. According to Lemma
48
.2, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P_{0}\in[P]
. Thus, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}(P_{0})\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{0})\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
. According to Lemma
48
.2,
[\theta_{X}(P)]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

If
[\theta_{X}(P)]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then, according to Lemma
48
.2, there exists a path
\theta_{X}(P)\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P_{0}\in[\theta_{X}(P)]
. Thus, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P^{\dagger}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P_{0}=\theta_{X}(P^{\dagger})
. According to Lemma
48
.1–2, there exists a path
[P]\Longrightarrow[P^{\dagger}]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
. Thus,
\theta_{X}([P])\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}([P^{\dagger}])\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
28
.

We are going to show that
\B:=\{(P,[P]),([P],P)\mid P\in\mathbb{P}^{g}\}
is a concrete branching time-out bisimulation up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
(see Definition
41
).

1.

•

Let
P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
and
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}
. If
\alpha\in A\vee P\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime\prime}
then, according to Lemma
48
.1,
[P]\xrightarrow{\alpha}[P^{\prime\prime}]
and, by definition of
\B
,
P^{\prime}\B[P^{\prime}]=[P]
and
P^{\prime\prime}\B[P^{\prime\prime}]
. Otherwise,
\alpha=\tau\wedge P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime\prime}
thus, by definition of
\B
,
P^{\prime\prime}\B[P^{\prime\prime}]=[P]
. In either case, there exists a path
[P]\xrightarrow{\mbox{\tiny\rm(}\alpha\mbox{\tiny\rm)}}[P^{\prime\prime}]
such that
P^{\prime}\B[P]
and
[P^{\prime\prime}]\B P^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If
[P]\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
and
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R^{\prime}
then, according to Lemma
48
.1,
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{2}
such that
P_{1}\in R^{\prime}
and
P_{2}\in R^{\prime\prime}
. Thus, by definition of
\B
,
P_{1}\B[P_{1}]=R^{\prime}
and
P_{2}\B[P_{2}]=R^{\prime\prime}
.

2.

•

Let
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{1}\wedge\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. By Lemma
48
.5 there exists a transition
[P]\mathbin{\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}}R^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))\B[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
. Moreover, by Lemma
48
.3,
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
since
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P_{1}\in[P]
.

•

Let
[P]\Longrightarrow R_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R_{2}
with
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R_{1}\wedge\mathcal{I}(R_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
. Then, according to Lemma
48
.4 and Corollary
49
, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P_{1}\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P_{2}
with
\mathcal{I}(P_{1})\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R_{2}))]
. Thus, applying Lemma
54
,
\theta_{X}(P_{2})\B[\theta_{X}(P_{2})]=[\theta_{X}(\chi(R_{2}))]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(R_{2})
.

3.

•

If
P\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P_{0}
then, according to Lemma
48
.2,
[P]\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

•

If
[P]\Longrightarrow R^{\prime}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
with
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R^{\prime}
then, according to Lemma
48
.1–2, there exists a path
P\Longrightarrow P^{\prime}\Longrightarrow P_{0}\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
such that
P^{\prime},P_{0}\in R^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix N
Completeness Proof by Canonical Representatives

Lemma 55
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathbb{P}^{g}
.

•

[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}[Q]\Rightarrow[P]=[Q]
.

•

\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}([Q])\Rightarrow\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[7.15547pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\theta_{X}([Q])
.

Proof.

•

If
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}[Q]
then, by Proposition
28
,
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}[Q]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q
. Thus,
[P]=[Q]
.

•

We are going to show that
{\B}:={\it Id}\cup\{(\theta_{X}([P]),\theta_{X}([Q]))\mid\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}([Q])\}
is a stability respecting branching bisimulation. Suppose
\theta_{X}([P])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}([Q])
. If
\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
then the first clause of Definition
1
suffices. If
\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
then
\mathcal{I}([P])\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
. As
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cX}_{br}\!}[Q]
, by Clause 2.c of Definition
1
there is a path
[Q]\Longrightarrow R
for some
R\in[\mathbb{P}^{g}]
with
[P]\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}R
. By the previous statement of this lemma,
R=[P]
. Thus
\theta_{X}([Q])\Longrightarrow\theta_{X}([P])\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
, which suffices to satisfy the first clause of Definition
7
. If
\theta_{X}([P])\,\not\!\xrightarrow{\tau}
then the stability-respecting clause of Definition
1
suffices.
∎

Proof of Proposition
30
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be a recursive specification such that
V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}:=\{y_{P^{\prime}}\mid P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)\}
and, for all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,
\mathcal{S}_{y_{P^{\prime}}}:=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.y_{P^{\prime\prime}}
. Note that
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
is strongly guarded since
P
is. We are going to show that
P
and
\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
are both
y_{P}
-components of solutions of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, so that the proposition follows by RSP.

First of all, consider
\rho:V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
such that
\forall P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P),\;\rho(y_{P^{\prime}}):=P^{\prime}
. For all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\rho(y_{P^{\prime}})=P^{\prime}=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.P^{\prime\prime}=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.\rho(y_{P^{\prime\prime}})
is a direct application of Lemma
24
. Thus, for all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\rho(y_{P^{\prime}})=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{y_{P^{\prime}}}[\rho]
, i.e.,
\rho
is a solution of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
, and
\rho(y_{P})=P
.

Next, consider
\nu:V_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
such that, for all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,

\displaystyle\nu(y_{P^{\prime}}):=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle

We are going to show that, for all
\alpha\in Act
and all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\alpha.\nu(y_{P^{\prime}})=\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle
. Let
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
.

•

If
\exists P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime\prime},\;P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime\prime}
then
\{(\alpha,[P^{\prime\prime}])\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\wedge(\alpha\in A\vee(\alpha=\tau\wedge P^{\prime}\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime\prime}))\}\subseteq\{(\alpha,R)\mid[P^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\alpha.\nu(y_{P^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\alpha.(\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\wedge\alpha\neq{\rm t}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)\qquad\qquad(\hyperlink{Lt}{\mbox{\bf L}\tau})

\displaystyle=\alpha.(\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\wedge(\alpha\in A\vee(\alpha=\tau\wedge P\,\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\underline{\makebox[5.58054pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{\!br}\!}\,P^{\prime}))\}}\hskip-50.0pt\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle+\tau.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{(branching axiom and RDP)}

•

If
\forall P\xrightarrow{\tau}P^{\prime},\,P\,\not\!\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}P^{\prime}
then, for all
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
,
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge P^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}\iff[P]\xrightarrow{\alpha}R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}([P])
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
then there exists a transition
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
with
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
. Thus
\theta_{X}([P^{\prime}])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
so
\theta_{X}([P^{\prime}])\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
by Lemma
55
, and therefore
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash{\rm t}.\theta_{X}([P^{\prime}])={\rm t}.\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
. Conversely, if
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
and
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
then there exists a transition
[P]\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}R^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\in[\theta_{X}(\chi(R^{\prime}))]
and thus
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash{\rm t}.\theta_{X}([P^{\prime}])={\rm t}.\theta_{X}(R^{\prime})
. Using the reactive approximation axiom,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\nu(y_{P^{\prime}})=\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle
and so, for all
\alpha\in Act
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\alpha.\nu(y_{P^{\prime}})=\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle
.

As a result, for all
P^{\prime}\in\mbox{Reach}(P)
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\nu(y_{P^{\prime}})=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime\prime}]}|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime\prime})\mid P^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime\prime}\}}\alpha.\nu(y_{P^{\prime\prime}})=\mathcal{S}_{y_{P^{\prime}}}[\nu]
, so
\nu
is a solution of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
up to
\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}
. Moreover,
\nu(y_{P})=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
which can be equated to
\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
by a single application of RDP.
∎

Proof of Theorem
31
.

According to Proposition
30
, it suffices to establish that
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\langle x_{Q}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
. By applying RDP, this amounts to proving that

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle

Let
(\alpha,P^{\prime})
such that
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}Q^{\prime}
. Thus,
[P^{\prime}]=[Q^{\prime}]
and so
\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle=\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle
. The same observation can be made for all
(\alpha,Q^{\prime})
such that
Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in A_{\tau}
. As a result,
\mathcal{I}(P)=\mathcal{I}(Q)
and

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle

Let
({\rm t},P^{\prime})
be such that
P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}
. Since
P\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{cr}_{br}\!}Q
, for all
X\subseteq A
such that
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, there exists a transition
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{X}(P^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(Q^{\prime})
. Thus,
recalling that
\langle x_{R}|\mathcal{S}\rangle\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,\!}R
for all
R
,
\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{c}_{br}\!}\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)
, so, by Lemma
55
,
\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)\linebreak\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{s}_{b}\!}\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)
and hence
{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)\mathrel{\,\raisebox{2.15277pt}{$\underline{\makebox[6.99997pt]{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\leftrightarrow$}}}$}\,^{sr}_{b}\!}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)
. Since
Ax^{\infty}
is a subset of
Ax^{\infty}_{r}
, according to Theorem
27
,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)={\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)
. The same observation can be made for all
({\rm t},Q^{\prime})
such that
Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}
. Let
X\subseteq A
. If
P\xrightarrow{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
then

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\alpha.\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle

\displaystyle=\psi_{X}(\langle x_{Q}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)

Otherwise,
\mathcal{I}(P)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
so
\mathcal{I}(Q)\cap(X\cup\{\tau\})=\emptyset
, thus,

\displaystyle Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\psi_{X}(\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{\alpha}P^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\!\!\alpha.\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle+\sum_{\{(t,P^{\prime})\mid P\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}P^{\prime}\}}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[P^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{(\alpha,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q^{\prime}\wedge\alpha\in A_{\tau}\}}\!\!\alpha.\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle+\sum_{\{(t,Q^{\prime})\mid Q\xrightarrow{{\rm t}}Q^{\prime}\}}{\rm t}.\theta_{X}(\langle x_{[Q^{\prime}]}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\psi_{X}(\langle x_{Q}\mid\mathcal{S}\rangle)

Using the reactive approximation axiom,
Ax^{\infty}_{r}\vdash\langle x_{P}|\mathcal{S}\rangle=\langle x_{Q}|\mathcal{S}\rangle
.
∎
